#### Arithmetic properties of the Hilbert representation

**Identifier:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/hilbert-uniqueness-determinant-oddness-irreducibility`; theorem.

For ρ_{π,λ} of R19.2/all-cohomological-hilbert-representation: (i) det ρ_{π,λ} = χ_{π,λ}·ε_ℓ^{−1}, equivalently det σ_λ(π) = χ_{π,λ}^{−1}ε_ℓ^{−1} in Carayol's normalisation (5.6.1); (ii) ρ_{π,λ} is totally odd: det ρ_{π,λ}(c_τ) = −1 for the complex conjugation c_τ at every real place τ; (iii) ρ_{π,λ} is absolutely irreducible; (iv) ρ_{π,λ} is determined up to isomorphism by its good-place polynomials, so it coincides with every construction having the same polynomials; in particular ρ_{π_{F′},λ} ≅ ρ_{π,λ}|G_{F′} for the base change π_{F′} to a totally real solvable F′/F when π_{F′} is cuspidal. Absolute irreducibility in characteristic zero does not imply absolute irreducibility of the residual representation.

**Hypotheses.** k_τ ≥ 2 and k_τ ≡ w mod 2; χ_π is algebraic with χ_{π,τ}(t) = sgn(t)^{k_τ}|t|^{−w}. A reducible ordinary local restriction at v | ℓ does not contradict (iii).

**Proof outline.** Determinant: in Carayol's case 5.6.1 gives det σ_λ(π) = χ_{π,λ}^{−1}ω^{−1} (R19.2/carayol-twisting-and-determinant), and ρ_{π,λ} = σ_λ(π) ⊗ χ_{π,λ}; in the congruence branch the determinant of the limit is the limit of the determinants. Both are the dictionary's (d). Oddness: the λ-adic character of χ_π takes the value (−1)^{k_τ}(−1)^{w} = 1 at c_τ (writing χ_π = φ|·|^{−w} with φ of finite order and φ_τ = sgn^{w}, the factor φ contributes (−1)^{w} = (−1)^{k_τ} and the algebraic part |·|^{−w} corresponds to ε_ℓ^{−w}, with value (−1)^{w} at c_τ; this is the formula for the value at complex conjugation in AutomorphicGaloisRepresentationsPartII AG2.0/galois-character-of-an-algebraic-hecke-character), and ε_ℓ(c_τ) = −1. Irreducibility (Skinner, Remark p. 256): if ρ_{π,λ} ⊗ Ē_λ ≅ χ₁ ⊕ χ₂, each χ_i is Hodge–Tate at a place above ℓ (R19.2/hilbert-hodge-tate-property), hence locally algebraic and de Rham there, and so the λ-adic character of an algebraic Hecke character ψ_i (PotentialModularityAndCompatibleSystems R24.5/character-system, after Serre III.2.3–2.4); over the totally real F each ψ_i is a finite-order character times an integral power of the norm. Then L(π ⊗ ψ₂^{−1}, s − 1/2) = L(ψ₁/ψ₂, s)ζ_F(s) up to finitely many Euler factors; writing ψ_i = ψ′_i|·|^{a_i} with a₁ ≥ a₂, L(ψ₁/ψ₂, 1) = L(ψ′₁/ψ′₂, a₁ − a₂ + 1) ≠ 0, so L(π ⊗ ψ₂^{−1}, s − 1/2) has a pole at s = 1 (the finitely many missing Euler factors have no zeros), that is, L(π ⊗ ψ₂^{−1}, s) has a pole at s = 1/2, contradicting cuspidality (AutomorphicLFunctionsAndLocalFactors AL.3). The source places the pole of L(π ⊗ ψ₂^{−1}, s) at s = 1 (source issue E5). Uniqueness and transfers: Čebotarev and Brauer–Nesbitt (ArithmeticGaloisRepresentations R01.1); for base change, the Satake parameters of π_{F′} at places above good v are those of π_v restricted (GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransfer R17.4).

**Acceptance.** At a real c_τ: trace 0 and determinant −1. Δ: det ρ_{π_Δ,λ} = ε_ℓ^{−11}, the value at a geometric Frobenius being p^{11}. 11a1 at λ = 5: the residual representation is reducible while ρ_{π_E,5} is irreducible.

**Direct inputs:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/all-cohomological-hilbert-representation`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/carayol-twisting-and-determinant`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/hilbert-normalisation-dictionary`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/hilbert-hodge-tate-property`, `ArithmeticGaloisRepresentations:R01.1`, `ArithmeticGaloisRepresentations:R01.5`, `GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransfer:R17.4`, `AutomorphicLFunctionsAndLocalFactors:AL.3`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentationsPartII:AG2.0/galois-character-of-an-algebraic-hecke-character`, `PotentialModularityAndCompatibleSystems:R24.5/character-system`.

**Source anchors:** [Henri Carayol](https://www.numdam.org/article/ASENS_1986_4_19_3_409_0.pdf), §3.1–3.3 and §5.5; [Christopher Skinner](https://content.ems.press/assets/public/full-texts/serials/dm/14/8965206/online/10.4171-dm-272.pdf), Remark, p. 256; [Takeshi Saito](https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0612077), §2, p. 9 of the arXiv version.

#### CM Hilbert eigenform

**Identifier:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/cm-hilbert-eigenform`; definition.

A regular cohomological Hilbert eigenform f over totally real F has CM if there is a totally imaginary quadratic extension L/F and a Hecke character α of L, of the infinity type producing f (at each τ its component is z^{(k_τ−1−w)/2} z̄^{(−k_τ+1−w)/2} or its conjugate, with half-integral exponents), such that π_f=AI_L^F(α), equivalently ρ_{π_f,λ} ≅ Ind_{G_L}^{G_F}(α′_λ) for every λ, where α′ = α·|·|_L^{−1/2} is algebraic because k_τ ≡ w mod 2, while α itself is not (Rec_v(AI(α)_v ⊗ |·|^{−1/2}) is induced from Rec_w(α_w ⊗ |·|_w^{−1/2})). Require α′_λ≠(α′_λ)^c so the induction is cuspidal and irreducible. This is an arithmetic predicate on the existing eigenform, importing automorphic induction and class-field characters; it is not another general induction construction.

**Hypotheses.** k_τ≥2; the weight-one analogue needs a separately stated finite-order character case. For Carayol's σ_λ(π_f) the inducing character is (α^{−1}|·|_L^{−1/2})_λ (his 11.3), and for the arithmetic dual ρ_{π_f,λ}^∨ it is (α′_λ)^{−1}; the definition does not depend on the choice.

**Proof outline.** Use R17.5 automorphic induction, and attach α′_λ to the algebraic Hecke character α′ (AutomorphicGaloisRepresentationsPartII AG2.0/galois-character-of-an-algebraic-hecke-character). The index-two induced-character formula matches traces at split primes and gives zero at inert primes. Strong multiplicity one and representation recognition prove equivalence of the automorphic and Galois formulations.

**Planning API.**

- `TauCeti.ModularGalois.IsCMHilbert` (characterisation): Existence of L/F and α with π_f=AI(α) and the matching induced Galois representation.
- `TauCeti.ModularGalois.IsCMHilbert.inducingField` (data): A CM quadratic inducing extension, recorded with its embedding and nontrivial automorphism.
- `TauCeti.ModularGalois.IsCMHilbert.trace_inert` (simp): Trace is zero at unramified primes inert in L.
- `TauCeti.ModularGalois.IsCMHilbert.trace_split` (simp): At a split prime the trace is α′_λ(Frob_w)+(α′_λ)^c(Frob_w), for geometric Frobenius elements.
- `TauCeti.ModularGalois.IsCMHilbert.twist` (functoriality): A compatible algebraic character twist preserves CM, with inducing character multiplied by its restriction.
- `TauCeti.ModularGalois.IsCMHilbert.coefficientExtension` (compatibility): CM is invariant under extending the coefficient field.

**Unit tests.**

- `TauCeti.ModularGalois.IsCMHilbert_Qi` (computation): The weight-two form of y²=x³−x, conductor 32, is induced from Q(i); unramified primes 3 mod 4 have trace zero.
- `TauCeti.ModularGalois.IsCMHilbert_twist` (characterisation): A finite-order character twist of this form has the same quadratic inducing field.
- `TauCeti.ModularGalois.IsCMHilbert_delta` (non-example): The level-one form Δ has no CM quadratic self-twist.
- `TauCeti.ModularGalois.IsCMHilbert_splitTrace` (computation): A split Frobenius acts diagonally by α′_λ and (α′_λ)^c; determinant is their product, whereas an inert Frobenius has trace zero.
- `TauCeti.ModularGalois.IsCMHilbert_halfIntegralShift` (computation): For y²=x³−x and 5=(2+i)(2−i): a_5=−2, and the values of α′_λ at the two geometric Frobenius elements above 5 are −1±2i, of absolute value 5^{1/2} and sum −2; the values of α or of α|·|_L^{−1} there have absolute value 1 or 5.

**Uses.** Dasgupta–Kakde §9.1: Separate the induced CM case from strongly irreducible representations. Dimitrov Proposition 3.8: Exclude persistent dihedral residual images for non-CM forms.

**Acceptance.** The weight-two theta series for Q(i) is CM; Δ of level one is non-CM.

**Direct inputs:** `GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransfer:R17.5`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/all-cohomological-hilbert-representation`, `ArithmeticGaloisRepresentations:R01.5`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/hilbert-normalisation-dictionary`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentationsPartII:AG2.0/galois-character-of-an-algebraic-hecke-character`.

**Source anchors:** [Samit Dasgupta and Mahesh Kakde](https://math.iisc.ac.in/~maheshkakde/brumerstark.pdf), §9.1, p. 66, CM definition; [Baskar Balasubramanyam, Eknath Ghate and Vinayak Vatsal](https://mathweb.tifr.res.in/~eghate/hilbertCM.pdf), Introduction, p. 2, Remarks (i)–(ii).

#### Virtual reducibility and CM

**Identifier:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/virtual-reducibility-implies-cm`; theorem. Planet: *CM and strong irreducibility*.

Let ρ be ρ_{π_f,λ} for a regular cohomological Hilbert eigenform f, or any twist or dual of it such as Carayol's σ_λ(π_f); it is absolutely irreducible. If its restriction to an open subgroup of G_F is reducible, then ρ is induced from a character of a quadratic extension L/F. Regular Hodge–Tate weights exclude finite projective image. The induced algebraic Hecke character and archimedean local Langlands then force L to be totally imaginary, so f is CM. Therefore a non-CM form remains irreducible on every open subgroup. The assertion that the inducing field is CM is restricted to k_τ≥2; weight-one dihedral forms need not satisfy the same archimedean argument.

**Hypotheses.** Continuous, semisimple, absolutely irreducible rank two; compact image over a finite λ-adic coefficient field. Two distinct labelled Hodge–Tate weights at each embedding give infinite projective image.

**Proof outline.** Pass to the normal core of the open subgroup. Clifford theory makes its restriction a sum of characters; its image is abelian. Ribet 1975 Theorem 2.3: an open abelian subgroup and infinite projective image give an abelian subgroup of index at most two; global irreducibility excludes index one. The two character lines are interchanged by the other coset, giving induction from the index-two subgroup. The inducing character is Hodge–Tate above ℓ (R19.2/hilbert-hodge-tate-property), so it is the λ-adic character of an algebraic Hecke character (PotentialModularityAndCompatibleSystems R24.5/character-system). BGV Remark (ii): a real place splitting in L would give an archimedean principal series, contradicting the discrete series of weight ≥2.

**Acceptance.** Non-CM implies strong irreducibility, not merely irreducibility over F. A finite-image weight-one representation does not satisfy the infinite-projective-image hypothesis.

**Direct inputs:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/hilbert-uniqueness-determinant-oddness-irreducibility`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/cm-hilbert-eigenform`, `ArithmeticGaloisRepresentations:R01.4`, `GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransfer:R17.5`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/hilbert-hodge-tate-property`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentationsPartII:AG2.0/galois-character-of-an-algebraic-hecke-character`, `PotentialModularityAndCompatibleSystems:R24.5/character-system`.

**Source anchors:** [Kenneth A. Ribet](https://math.berkeley.edu/~ribet/Articles/invent_28.pdf), Theorem 2.3 and proof, printed pp. 251–252; [Baskar Balasubramanyam, Eknath Ghate and Vinayak Vatsal](https://mathweb.tifr.res.in/~eghate/hilbertCM.pdf), Remark (ii), p. 2; [Samit Dasgupta and Mahesh Kakde](https://math.iisc.ac.in/~maheshkakde/brumerstark.pdf), §9.1, Lemma 9.1, p. 66, with its proof on p. 67.

#### Ordinary CM primes split

**Identifier:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/ordinary-cm-primes-split`; theorem.

For a regular cohomological CM Hilbert form f=AI_L^F(α) that is p-ordinary at every v|p, each v|p splits in the CM quadratic extension L/F, and the inducing character has a p-ordinary CM type compatible with the chosen embeddings. For the Hara–Ochiai converse retain the source’s p-ordinary CM-type/infinity-type hypothesis; splitting alone without that hypothesis is not the stated iff criterion.

**Hypotheses.** CM is a hypothesis of Hara–Ochiai Proposition A.3; the theorem is not for arbitrary ordinary Hilbert forms. Regular weights make the two inducing local characters distinct; arbitrary index-two induction need not be locally irreducible.

**Proof outline.** At a nonsplit v, restrict the induced representation to G_Fv; regular labelled Hodge types distinguish α from its local conjugate, so induction is irreducible. The R21.3 ordinary triangular theorem makes that local representation reducible, a contradiction. Use the valuation/infinity-type calculation in Hara–Ochiai to identify the p-ordinary CM type and the converse under its full hypotheses.

**Acceptance.** For Q(i), good p=5 splits and can be ordinary; p=3 is inert and the weight-two CM form is nonordinary. An ordinary non-CM form has no CM extension to which this conclusion applies.

**Direct inputs:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/cm-hilbert-eigenform`, `OrdinaryAutomorphicFormsAndModularityLifting:R21.3`, `GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransfer:R17.5`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/hilbert-hodge-tate-property`.

**Source anchors:** [Takashi Hara and Tadashi Ochiai](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1507.07309v2), Appendix A Proposition A.3 and proof, pp. 70–71; §2.1 (ord); [Samit Dasgupta and Mahesh Kakde](https://math.iisc.ac.in/~maheshkakde/brumerstark.pdf), Lemma 9.2 proof, p. 67.

#### Complex conjugation moves the CM ordinary line

**Identifier:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/cm-ordinary-line-complex-conjugation`; theorem.

Dasgupta–Kakde Lemma 9.2: for a regular p-ordinary CM Hilbert eigenform induced from L/F and v|p, the specified ordinary line V_v,f is not stable under ρ_f(τ) for any τ∈G_F restricting to the nontrivial complex conjugation on L. Since v splits, G_Fv⊂G_L. With distinct ordinary local characters, the ordinary line is one of the two global G_L-character lines, and τ interchanges them.

**Hypotheses.** Retain k>1 and the distinct local characters used in the source (one ramified, one unramified in its setup). An arbitrary invariant line when the two local characters coincide does not satisfy this argument.

**Proof outline.** Apply ordinary-cm-primes-split and the induced representation decomposition over G_L. Distinctness makes the specified ordinary line one of the two character eigenspaces. An element in the other coset swaps the eigenspaces. If it fixed one, that line would be G_F-stable, contradicting absolute irreducibility.

**Acceptance.** In a two-coset induced basis τ has an off-diagonal matrix and sends each CM line to the other. Coincident local characters invalidate the eigenspace identification.

**Direct inputs:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/ordinary-cm-primes-split`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/hilbert-uniqueness-determinant-oddness-irreducibility`, `OrdinaryAutomorphicFormsAndModularityLifting:R21.3`.

**Source anchors:** [Samit Dasgupta and Mahesh Kakde](https://math.iisc.ac.in/~maheshkakde/brumerstark.pdf), Lemma 9.2 and proof, p. 67.

### R19.3 specifications

#### Saito's weight–monodromy theorem for the geometric eigenform realisations

**Identifier:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.3/strict-compatibility-and-the-monodromy-weight-purity`; theorem.

Saito's Theorem 2, in his labelling and in this roadmap's: let F be totally real of degree g > 1 and f a Hilbert eigen-newform of multiweight (k_τ, w_S) with w_S ≥ k_τ ≥ 2 and k_τ ≡ w_S mod 2, and if g is even assume f has a finite discrete-series component. For Saito's ρ_{f,λ} = σ̌_h(π_f), which is Carayol's σ_λ(π_f) for π_f of infinity type (k, 2 − w_S) (R19.2/hilbert-normalisation-dictionary), and a finite place p of F, the monodromy filtrations of the Weil–Deligne representations of ρ_{f,λ}|G_{F_p} (λ ∤ p) and of D_pst(ρ_{f,μ}|G_{F_p}) (μ | p) are pure of weight w_S − 1: for a geometric Frobenius lift, N = 0 gives eigenvalues of weight w_S − 1, and N ≠ 0 gives weight w_S − 2 on ker N and w_S on coker N. Equivalently ρ_{π_f,λ} = σ_λ(π_f) ⊗ χ_{π_f,λ} is pure of weight 3 − w_S = w + 1. The classical case F = Q, f of weight k, is Saito 2000, Theorems 1 and 3 (weight k − 1 for M_{f,λ}). This geometric theorem is the input to Saito's coefficient-prime comparison; purity for every Hilbert form is R19.3/monodromy-weight-purity-of-the-family.

**Hypotheses.** Saito's setting: g > 1, w_S ≥ k_τ ≥ 2, k_τ ≡ w_S mod 2, and a finite discrete-series place when g is even; the condition w_S ≥ k_τ is his choice of twist, not a restriction on π. N² = 0, and the monodromy filtration 0 ⊂ Im N ⊂ Ker N ⊂ V is the one Saito uses. Weil–Deligne relation F N F^{−1} = q^{−1}N for a geometric Frobenius F; Saito's printed reversed relation is PadicHodgeTheory/E50.

**Proof outline.** The case N ≠ 0 follows from the determinant and the isomorphism N: Gr^W_1(V)(1) ≅ Gr^W_{−1}(V) (Saito's Remark). For N = 0: realise ρ_{f,λ} in the cohomology of the semistable model of Saito's Kuga–Sato analogue over the Shimura curve (WeightsInEtaleCohomology R34.3/saito-semistable-comparison-model); the weight spectral sequence, the Weil conjectures for the components of the special fibre and the vanishing of the relevant global sections (Saito Proposition 1, §10) give the weights (WeightsInEtaleCohomology R34.6, requested as a geometric statement on these realisations). The same argument with log-crystalline cohomology gives the statement for D_pst; dualising and twisting by χ_{π_f,λ} translates the weight by the dictionary.

**Acceptance.** Steinberg place of a weight-two form (w_S = 2): eigenvalues of weights 0 and 2 occur, so N ≠ 0 is detected by weights. 11a1 at 11: Saito's ρ is H¹_ét(E_Q̄, Q_ℓ) with geometric Frobenius eigenvalues 1 on ker N and 11 on coker N.

**Direct inputs:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/carayol-sigma-lambda-construction`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/hilbert-normalisation-dictionary`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/hilbert-modular-compatible-system-carayol-theorem-A`, `WeightsInEtaleCohomology:R34.3/saito-semistable-comparison-model`, `WeightsInEtaleCohomology:R34.6`, `WeightsInEtaleCohomology:R34.6/arithmetic-realization-transport`, `ArithmeticGaloisRepresentations:R01.2`.

#### Ramanujan conjecture for Hilbert modular forms

**Identifier:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.3/hilbert-ramanujan-conjecture`; theorem. Planet: *Ramanujan conjecture for Hilbert forms*.

Let π be a cuspidal automorphic representation of GL₂(A_F), F totally real of degree d, of infinity type (k,w): for every real place τ, π_τ is the discrete series D_{k_τ,w} with Blattner parameter k_τ and central character t ↦ sgn(t)^{k_τ}|t|^{−w} (Carayol 0.2–0.3; Skinner §1), with k_τ ≥ 2 and k_τ ≡ w mod 2 for all τ. Then for every finite place v the unitary twist π_v ⊗ |det|_v^{w/2} is tempered; equivalently each irreducible constituent Λ of the Weil–Deligne representation Rec_v(π_v ⊗ |det|_v^{w/2}) has bounded image (Blasius §2.2). At v ∉ S_π this says: for every embedding ι of E into C, both roots α of X² − t_vX + q_vs_v satisfy |ι(α)| = q_v^{(w+1)/2}, so |ι(t_v)| ≤ 2q_v^{(w+1)/2}. For F = Q and π_f of infinity type (k, k−2) this is Deligne's bound |a_p| ≤ 2p^{(k−1)/2}.

**Hypotheses.** Weights all congruent modulo 2 and at least 2: Blasius's type MC; parallel and non-parallel weights are both covered. The Satake parameters are those of the positive-uniformiser operators of R19.2/all-cohomological-hilbert-representation.

**Proof outline.** Reduction to unramified places (Blasius §2.2): a supercuspidal or Steinberg-twist component of a unitary cuspidal representation is tempered; a ramified principal series ψ₁ ⊕ ψ₂ with unitary product is either tempered or of complementary type μψ ⊕ μ^{−1}ψ with ψ of finite order. A finite-order idele class character η with η_v = ψ exists (the Grunwald–Wang theorem, requested from GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransfer R17.5); π ⊗ η^{−1} has the same infinity type and is unramified-twisted at v, so it suffices to treat places where π is unramified. Motivic cases (Blasius §4.1 with Proposition 5): if some k_τ > 2, or d is odd, or π has a finite discrete-series place, ρ_{π,λ} occurs up to twist in the cohomology of a smooth projective variety X (Carayol's Shimura curves and Saito's Kuga–Sato analogues, or Blasius–Rogawski motives on unitary Shimura varieties, AutomorphicGaloisRepresentationsPartII AG2.1). At the places of good reduction of X, Deligne's Weil I (WeightsInEtaleCohomology R34.5) makes the Frobenius eigenvalues pure of weight w + 1. At an arbitrary unramified v, de Jong's theorem (Blasius Proposition 2, WeightsInEtaleCohomology R34.4) makes the Frobenius eigenvalues of ρ_{π,λ}|G_{F_v} Weil numbers of integral weight, while the classification of unitary representations (GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransfer R16.2) puts the weights of the unitary twist strictly between −1 and 1; hence the weight is exactly w + 1, and the good-place polynomial transfers this to the Satake parameters. Parallel weight two, d even, principal series everywhere (Blasius §4.2): after a totally real quadratic base change where needed (Blasius's Remark, GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransfer R17.4), take the quaternion algebra B split at exactly two real places τ₁, τ₂ and at all finite places. By Proposition 6 (Reimann; Brylinski–Labesse; HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves R18.4), the constituents of H²(Sh_{B,W}, Q_ℓ)(π′_f) over Γ_L are those of R_ℓ(π) = ρ_{π,λ}|_L ⊗ ^{τ₂}ρ_{π,λ}|_L. The weight–monodromy conjecture holds for H² of surfaces (Rapoport–Zink with de Jong's alterations, WeightsInEtaleCohomology R34.4), Λ²ρ_{π,λ} = det ρ_{π,λ} is pure, and the tensor-product lemma of Blasius §4.2 makes each factor pure: the Frobenius eigenvalues of ρ_{π,λ} at v have weight w + 1. Transfer to π_v: by the good-place polynomial (R19.2/all-cohomological-hilbert-representation) the Satake parameters of π_v ⊗ |det|^{w/2} have absolute value 1 under every embedding.

**Acceptance.** 11a1 at 2: X² + 2X + 2 has roots of absolute value √2 = 2^{(0+1)/2}. Δ at 2: X² + 24X + 2048 has negative discriminant 576 − 8192, and |α|² = 2^{11}. Non-example: a complementary-series local component μψ ⊕ μ^{−1}ψ with μ = |·|^t, 0 < t < 1/2, does not occur.

**Direct inputs:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/all-cohomological-hilbert-representation`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/hilbert-normalisation-dictionary`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/carayol-sigma-lambda-construction`, `WeightsInEtaleCohomology:R34.5`, `WeightsInEtaleCohomology:R34.4`, `HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves:R18.4`, `GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransfer:R17.4`, `GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransfer:R16.2`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentationsPartII:AG2.1`, `GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransfer:R17.5`, `ArithmeticGaloisRepresentations:R01.2`.

**Source anchors:** [Don Blasius](https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0511007), Theorem 1, p. 1 (arXiv v1); [Don Blasius](https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0511007), §2.2, p. 15; [Don Blasius](https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0511007), §4.2, p. 21; [Christopher Skinner](https://content.ems.press/assets/public/full-texts/serials/dm/14/8965206/online/10.4171-dm-272.pdf), §2.4.1, Remark, p. 252; [Don Blasius](https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0511007), Proposition 2, p. 11, and Proposition 5, pp. 16–17.

#### Weight–monodromy purity of every Hilbert representation

**Identifier:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.3/monodromy-weight-purity-of-the-family`; theorem. Planet: *Weight–monodromy purity*.

Let π be a cuspidal automorphic representation of GL₂(A_F), F totally real of degree d, of infinity type (k,w): for every real place τ, π_τ is the discrete series D_{k_τ,w} with Blattner parameter k_τ and central character t ↦ sgn(t)^{k_τ}|t|^{−w} (Carayol 0.2–0.3; Skinner §1), with k_τ ≥ 2 and k_τ ≡ w mod 2 for all τ, λ | ℓ, and v a finite place of F. If v ∤ ℓ, the Frobenius-semisimple Weil–Deligne representation WD(ρ_{π,λ}|G_{F_v})^{F-ss} is pure of weight w + 1 in the monodromy sense: either N = 0 and every eigenvalue of a geometric Frobenius lift is a q_v-Weil number of weight w + 1 under every embedding, or N ≠ 0, ker N has weight w and coker N has weight w + 2. If v | ℓ, the same holds for the Weil–Deligne representation of D_pst(ρ_{π,λ}|G_{F_v}). In Carayol's and Saito's normalisation σ_λ(π) = ρ_{π,λ} ⊗ χ_{π,λ}^{−1} the weight is 1 − w = w_S − 1.

**Hypotheses.** N is normalised by F N F^{−1} = q_v^{−1}N for a geometric Frobenius F (ArithmeticGaloisRepresentations R01.2). No discrete-series place and no residual hypothesis.

**Proof outline.** Away from ℓ: by R19.4/all-hilbert-local-global-compatibility, WD(ρ_{π,λ}|G_{F_v})^{F-ss} ≅ ιRec_v(π_v ⊗ |·|_v^{−1/2}) = ιRec_v(π′_v) ⊗ |·|_v^{−(w+1)/2} with π′ = π ⊗ |det|^{w/2}. By R19.3/hilbert-ramanujan-conjecture π′_v is tempered, so Rec_v(π′_v) is a sum of Λ ⊗ V_t with Λ bounded (Blasius §2.2, types (i)–(iii)): weight 0 in the monodromy sense. The unramified twist |·|^{−(w+1)/2} takes the value q_v^{(w+1)/2} at a geometric Frobenius, which shifts all weights by w + 1 (Blasius Proposition 1). For N ≠ 0, π_v is a twist ψ·St and the parameter is ψ|·|^{−(w+1)/2} ⊗ V_{q^{1/2},1}; the relation FNF^{−1} = q^{−1}N puts ker N in weight w and coker N in weight w + 2. This is Blasius's Corollary 7 and Proposition 5 argument. At v | ℓ: R19.5/skinner-full-hilbert-coefficient-prime identifies the Weil–Deligne representation of D_pst with the same ιRec_v(π_v ⊗ |·|_v^{−1/2}); apply the previous steps.

**Acceptance.** 11a1 at 11 (split multiplicative, infinity type (2,0)): N ≠ 0, geometric Frobenius acts by 1 on ker N (weight 0 = w) and by 11 on coker N (weight 2 = w + 2). Δ at any p: N = 0 and the eigenvalues have weight 11 = w + 1. The split semisimplification of a Steinberg parameter has the same Frobenius eigenvalues of weights w and w + 2 with N = 0; it is not pure, which is how purity detects N.

**Direct inputs:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.4/all-hilbert-local-global-compatibility`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.5/skinner-full-hilbert-coefficient-prime`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.3/hilbert-ramanujan-conjecture`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/hilbert-normalisation-dictionary`, `ArithmeticGaloisRepresentations:R01.2`, `GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransfer:R16.3`.

**Source anchors:** [Don Blasius](https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0511007), Corollary 7, p. 20; [Don Blasius](https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0511007), Proposition 5, p. 16; [Takeshi Saito](https://www.ms.u-tokyo.ac.jp/~t-saito/pp/weightmonodromy.pdf), Theorem 1, printed p. 429 (page image).

#### Absolute irreducibility of the representation of a classical newform

**Identifier:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.3/classical-newform-irreducibility`; theorem.

Let f be a normalised newform of weight k ≥ 2, level N and character ψ, with coefficient field K_f. For every finite place λ of K_f the representation ρ_{f,λ} = M_{f,λ}^∨ of R19.1/newform-rank-two-realisation is absolutely irreducible. (Weight one is R19.1/weight-one-cuspidal-irreducibility.)

**Hypotheses.** k ≥ 2: the Hodge–Tate weights 0 and k − 1 are distinct. No condition on λ; residual irreducibility is a different statement.

**Proof outline.** Suppose ρ_{f,λ} ⊗ K̄_{f,λ} is reducible, with Jordan–Hölder characters χ₁ and χ₂ (no semisimplicity is assumed). The restriction of ρ_{f,λ} to G_{Q_ℓ} is Hodge–Tate with weights 0 and k − 1, and so are its subquotients: M_{f,λ} is cut out of the cohomology of a smooth projective variety over Q by the Scholl projector (k ≥ 3) or the weight-two Jacobian factor (k = 2) (R19.1/scholl-projector, R19.1/newform-projector-and-coefficient-descent), and Faltings' Hodge–Tate decomposition applies (PadicHodgeTheory R06.5). Each χ_i is then a Hodge–Tate character of G_Q unramified outside Nℓ, hence locally algebraic and of the form ε_ℓ^{n_i}η_i with η_i of finite order (PotentialModularityAndCompatibleSystems R24.5/character-system: it is the ℓ-adic character of an algebraic Hecke character of Q, a finite-order character times a power of the norm), and {n₁, n₂} = {0, k − 1}. For p ∤ Nℓ, the trace gives a_p = η₁(Frob_p)p^{n₁} + η₂(Frob_p)p^{n₂}, so |a_p| ≥ p^{k−1} − 1 under every embedding, which exceeds 2p^{(k−1)/2} for p large; this contradicts the bound of WeightsInEtaleCohomology R34.6/eigenform-purity-and-ramanujan-bound. Equivalently, Skinner's Remark (p. 256) applies with F = Q: a decomposition would give L(f ⊗ η₂^{−1}, s) a pole. The Hilbert irreducibility node R19.2/hilbert-uniqueness-determinant-oddness-irreducibility is not used, since the Hilbert construction depends on the classical realisation.

**Acceptance.** Δ: ρ_{Δ,ℓ} is irreducible for every ℓ, although ρ̄_{Δ,691} is reducible (Ramanujan's congruence τ(n) ≡ σ₁₁(n) mod 691). 11a1: irreducible at every λ, residually reducible at 5. Non-example: the Eisenstein series E_k gives χ_ℓ^{k−1} ⊕ 1, Hodge–Tate with weights 0, k − 1; it is excluded because it is not cuspidal, and the bound |a_p| ≤ 2p^{(k−1)/2} fails for it.

**Direct inputs:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.1/newform-rank-two-realisation`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.1/scholl-projector`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.1/newform-projector-and-coefficient-descent`, `PadicHodgeTheory:R06.5`, `PotentialModularityAndCompatibleSystems:R24.5/character-system`, `WeightsInEtaleCohomology:R34.6/eigenform-purity-and-ramanujan-bound`.

**Source anchors:** [Christopher Skinner](https://content.ems.press/assets/public/full-texts/serials/dm/14/8965206/online/10.4171-dm-272.pdf), Remark, p. 256; [H. Darmon, F. Diamond and R. Taylor](https://www.math.mcgill.ca/darmon/pub/Articles/Expository/05.DDT/paper.pdf), Theorem 3.1(a)–(c), p. 86 (revision of 9 September 2007).

#### Dimitrov’s residual large-image theorem

**Identifier:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.3/dimitrov-large-image`; theorem. Planet: *Dimitrov large image*.

For a fixed non-CM regular cohomological Hilbert eigenform f, for all but finitely many rational primes ℓ and each relevant λ|ℓ, after conjugacy the residual image contains SL₂(F_q) for a finite subfield F_q⊆κ_λ, q=ℓ^a, and lies in κ_λ^×GL₂(F_q). In particular it contains a conjugate of SL₂(F_ℓ). Keep the finite exceptional set depending on f, its level, weights and coefficient field. Do not assert full GL₂(κ_λ).

**Hypotheses.** Non-CM and k_τ≥2; finite bad set includes coefficient, weight and level exceptions. Dimitrov’s integral/residual setup and coefficient embeddings are retained; the statement is uniform outside a finite set of rational characteristics.

**Proof outline.** Proposition 3.1 excludes residual reducibility outside a finite set using class field theory, labelled inertia and global units. Proposition 3.5 excludes infinitely many dihedral images: finitely many quadratic fields/characters and congruences force a CM theta series. §3.2 excludes exceptional projective images using tame-inertia orders as ℓ grows. Apply Dickson’s classification to obtain Proposition 3.8 and the displayed subfield form.

**Acceptance.** For finitely many fixed forms, union their exceptional sets before choosing a common large ℓ. A CM form has persistent dihedral projective image and is excluded.

**Direct inputs:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/cm-hilbert-eigenform`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/hilbert-uniqueness-determinant-oddness-irreducibility`, `ArithmeticGaloisRepresentations:R01.4`, `FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheory:R07.3`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.3/fixed-eigenform-compatible-family`.

**Source anchors:** [Mladen Dimitrov](https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0411152), §3.1 Proposition 3.1; §3.3 Proposition 3.5; §3.4 Proposition 3.8; [James Newton and Jack A. Thorne](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2212.03595), Proof of Lemma 5.7, printed p. 41.

#### Ribet–Momose large image for classical forms

**Identifier:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.3/ribet-momose-classical-large-image`; theorem. Planet: *Ribet–Momose large image*.

For a normalised non-CM classical cuspidal newform f of weight k≥2, the Zariski closure of ρ_f,λ contains SL₂ over the algebraic closure of K_f,λ for every λ. Its residual representation is absolutely irreducible for all but finitely many λ, and its residual image contains a conjugate of SL₂(F_ℓ) for all but finitely many rational ℓ, in particular for the large split primes used by Newton–Thorne. Inner twists may constrain the coefficient field and determinant: the open image is described using the inner-twist fixed field and its quaternion algebra, not automatically GL₂(O_K,λ).

**Hypotheses.** Characteristic-zero statement holds for every λ; residual statement excludes a finite set. Keep f fixed and non-CM.

**Proof outline.** Ribet 1985 §3 recalls Momose’s theorem: on the kernel of the inner twists the image is open in the reduced-norm-constrained unit group of a quaternion algebra over the fixed field. After extending coefficients to an algebraic closure, its derived group gives SL₂. Theorem 2.1 proves residual irreducibility and divisibility of image order by ℓ, using the conductors of twists (its Corollary 1.2, from local–global compatibility, R19.4/conductor-and-local-factors-classical) and the shape of the residual representation on inertia at ℓ (FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheory R07.3); Theorem 3.1 upgrades this to the prescribed large image for almost all ℓ by finite-group classification and lifting commutators.

**Acceptance.** The theorem applies to Δ at every λ in characteristic zero; it makes no assertion about each small residual prime. A CM theta series has torus-normaliser Zariski closure and fails the SL₂ conclusion.

**Direct inputs:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.1/newform-rank-two-realisation`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/cm-hilbert-eigenform`, `ArithmeticGaloisRepresentations:R01.4`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.3/fixed-eigenform-compatible-family`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.3/classical-newform-irreducibility`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.4/conductor-and-local-factors-classical`, `FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheory:R07.3`.

**Source anchors:** [Kenneth A. Ribet](https://math.berkeley.edu/~ribet/Articles/rankin.pdf), Theorem 2.1 pp. 188–190; §3 and Theorem 3.1 pp. 190–192; [James Newton and Jack A. Thorne](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2009.07180v2), Proof of Theorem 3.1, printed p. 26.

#### Compatible family of a fixed eigenform

**Identifier:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.3/fixed-eigenform-compatible-family`; construction. Planet: *Eigenform compatible family*.

For a fixed classical newform f, through π_f of infinity type (k, k − 2), or a fixed cuspidal π of infinity type (k,w) over a totally real F as in R19.2/all-cohomological-hilbert-representation, place the family {ρ_{π,λ}}_λ, indexed by the finite places λ of one coefficient field E ⊇ Q(π), in the early compatible-system carrier of R24.5:operations with: coefficient field E; ramification set S_π; good-place polynomials P_v(X) = X² − t_vX + q_vs_v ∈ E[X] for v ∉ S_π, independent of λ ∤ q_v; Hodge degrees (w − k_τ + 2)/2 and (w + k_τ)/2 at each τ; and at every finite v the Frobenius-semisimple Weil–Deligne parameter ιRec_v(π_v ⊗ |·|_v^{−1/2}), for v ∤ ℓ by R19.4/all-hilbert-local-global-compatibility and for v | ℓ by R19.5/skinner-full-hilbert-coefficient-prime. The family is strictly compatible and pure of weight w + 1 at every finite place (R19.3/monodromy-weight-purity-of-the-family). With Kisin's theorem alone the coefficient-prime assertion is available at the λ with absolutely irreducible residual representation, and with Saito's alone under his degree and discrete-series hypotheses; a family known only by its good-place polynomials is weakly compatible.

**Hypotheses.** Only regular weight k_τ ≥ 2 is in this family; finite-image weight-one systems use R19.1/weight-one-artin-representation. One normalisation, ρ_{π,λ}, at every λ; Carayol's or Saito's family is σ_λ(π) = ρ_{π,λ} ⊗ χ_{π,λ}^{−1}, itself strictly compatible. The carrier and the weak, almost strict and strict predicates belong to PotentialModularityAndCompatibleSystems R24.5:operations.

**Proof outline.** Construct each member by R19.2/all-cohomological-hilbert-representation; for F = Q identify it with M_{f,λ} (R19.1/newform-rank-two-realisation, dictionary (f)). No potential-modularity existence theorem is used. Read the coefficients of P_v from the Hecke eigenvalues in E; attach the common WD parameters away from λ and at λ by the two compatibility theorems. Compare Hodge data, determinants and coefficient changes; fill the imported predicates to the strength these inputs establish, and record purity from R19.3/monodromy-weight-purity-of-the-family.

**Planning API.**

- `TauCeti.ModularGalois.fixedEigenformFamily` (constructor): The compatible family {ρ_{π,λ}}_λ over one coefficient field E with ramification set S_π.
- `TauCeti.ModularGalois.fixedEigenformFamily_member` (projection): Its λ-member is ρ_{π,λ} of R19.2/all-cohomological-hilbert-representation.
- `TauCeti.ModularGalois.fixedEigenformFamily_goodPolynomial` (data): P_v(X) = X² − t_vX + q_vs_v ∈ E[X] for v ∉ S_π.
- `TauCeti.ModularGalois.fixedEigenformFamily_localParameter` (data): At every finite v the parameter ιRec_v(π_v ⊗ |·|_v^{−1/2}), with N.
- `TauCeti.ModularGalois.fixedEigenformFamily_coeffChange` (functoriality): Enlarging E or conjugating by σ ∈ Aut(C) gives the family of π^σ.
- `TauCeti.ModularGalois.fixedEigenformFamily_strict` (characterisation): The family satisfies the strict predicate of R24.5:operations and is pure of weight w + 1.

**Unit tests.**

- `TauCeti.ModularGalois.fixedEigenformFamily_delta` (computation): For Δ (infinity type (12,10)): P_2(X) = X² + 24X + 2048 for every λ ∤ 2, and the family is pure of weight 11.
- `TauCeti.ModularGalois.fixedEigenformFamily_steinberg` (compatibility): For 11a1 at v = 11 the parameter is the twist of Steinberg with N ≠ 0, for every λ ∤ 11, and agrees with the Tate-curve representation H¹_ét.
- `TauCeti.ModularGalois.fixedEigenformFamily_weakNotStrict` (non-example): The family defined only by its good polynomials, without the local parameters at bad places and at λ, is weakly but not certified strictly compatible.
- `TauCeti.ModularGalois.fixedEigenformFamily_evenHilbert` (degenerate): For d even and π principal series everywhere the strict family still exists; it does not rely on a discrete-series place.

**Uses.** PotentialModularityAndCompatibleSystems R24: Input an actual fixed modular-form family into the generic operations. Newton–Thorne prime selection; R19.3/dimitrov-large-image: Compare residual images of chosen members without changing the eigenform.

**Acceptance.** Good places have the same P_v for all λ not above v. An almost strictly compatible lifting system remains weaker than the strict fixed modular-form family.

**Direct inputs:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/all-cohomological-hilbert-representation`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/hilbert-normalisation-dictionary`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.1/newform-rank-two-realisation`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.4/all-hilbert-local-global-compatibility`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.5/skinner-full-hilbert-coefficient-prime`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.3/monodromy-weight-purity-of-the-family`, `PotentialModularityAndCompatibleSystems:R24.5:operations`.

#### Density-one ordinary primes in weight two

**Identifier:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.3/skinner-density-one-ordinary-primes`; theorem.

For the non-CM weight-two newform f of squarefree level N and trivial nebentypus in Skinner 2020 §3, the rational primes p∤N for which f is ordinary at some λ|p have density one. For all sufficiently large such p, every λ|p has absolutely irreducible residual representation, and that residual representation is ramified at every q|N. Thus one may choose p≥5 and λ|p ordinary while retaining all these residual conditions. The quantifier for ordinarity is some λ, while the eventual irreducibility/ramification statements hold for every λ.

**Hypotheses.** Fixed non-CM weight-two form; retain squarefree N and trivial character for this routed level-lowering argument. Exclude finitely many coefficient ramification and small primes.

**Proof outline.** If a_p is nonunit at every λ|p and p is unramified in K_f, Norm(a_p) is an integer divisible by p^[K_f:Q]. Ramanujan bounds its absolute value by (2√p)^[K_f:Q], forcing a_p=0 for p>4. Serre’s trace-zero density theorem and non-CM large image make a_p=0 a density-zero set; this gives density-one ordinarity at some λ. Use Ribet 1985 Theorem 2.1 for eventual residual absolute irreducibility. If residual ramification at q|N failed for infinitely many large p, R20.2 level lowering and finiteness of weight-two lower-level forms produce one g congruent to f in infinitely many characteristics. Its good coefficients equal those of f; multiplicity one contradicts its lower conductor.

**Acceptance.** The conclusion is not ordinarity at every λ|p. Squarefree-level minimal ramification is not asserted for an arbitrary level/type.

### R19.4 specifications

#### Local-global compatibility away from p, in the Hecke normalization, with its exact relation to Langlands

**Identifier:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.4/hecke-versus-langlands-normalization-and-local-global-compatibility`; theorem. Planet: *Local–global compatibility away from p*.

For each finite place p of F, Kutzko's work attaches to pi_p a Frobenius-semisimple degree-2 representation sigma(pi_p) of the local Weil-Deligne group W'_p, characterized by L(chi . sigma-check(pi_p)) = L(chi . omega_p^{1/2} . pi_p) and eps(chi . sigma-check(pi_p)) = eps(chi . omega_p^{1/2} . pi_p) for every quasicharacter chi of F_p^*, where sigma-check denotes the contragredient. This 'Hecke' correspondence differs from the Langlands correspondence by a twist by omega_p^{1/2} followed by passage to the contragredient. For an irreducible principal series pi_p = Ind(xi_1, xi_2) (unitary induction) one has sigma(pi_p) = xi_1^{-1} omega_p^{-1/2} + xi_2^{-1} omega_p^{-1/2}. In all cases (a) det sigma(pi_p) = chi_{pi_p}^{-1} omega_p^{-1}, where chi_{pi_p} is the central character of pi_p, and (b) sigma(chi . pi_p) = chi^{-1} . sigma(pi_p). Its advantage over Langlands' correspondence is compatibility with automorphisms of the field of definition. By 0.6 there is a finite extension E of Q(pi) over which all sigma(pi_p) are realized, giving for each finite lambda of E of residue characteristic different from that of p a lambda-adic representation sigma^lambda(pi_p) of W_{F_p}; Carayol's Theorem (A) asserts that the restriction of sigma_lambda to W_{F_p} is EQUIVALENT to sigma^lambda(pi_p). Since sigma^lambda(pi_p) comes from the Weil-Deligne representation sigma(pi_p), this includes the monodromy operator and hence the special/Steinberg case, not only the semisimplified inertia action or the unramified traces.

**Hypotheses.** the comparison is at finite places p whose residue characteristic differs from that of lambda. sigma(pi_p) is F-semisimple by construction, and Theorem (A) asserts an equivalence of lambda-adic representations of W_{F_p}, not merely an isomorphism after F-semisimplification. the normalization of class field theory is fixed so that GEOMETRIC Frobenius elements correspond to uniformizers (source 0.4) the twist relating Hecke to Langlands is by omega_p^{1/2} together with contragredience (0.5); other papers use other normalizations under the same name (Saito's sigma_h, recalled from [De], appears in his Theorem 0 as sigma-check_h, which is Carayol's sigma: R19.2/hilbert-normalisation-dictionary (b)) Translation to this roadmap's global object: Carayol's σ_λ(π) = ρ_{π,λ} ⊗ χ_{π,λ}^{−1} (R19.2/hilbert-normalisation-dictionary (a)).

**Proof outline.** Kutzko's classification of the local representations gives sigma(pi_p) with the stated L- and epsilon-factor characterization. The source records properties (a) and (b) as consequences, and notes that the compatibility with field automorphisms is what makes the Hecke normalization preferable for rationality questions. Carayol's Theorem (A) supplies the global-to-local comparison at every finite place. Applied to F = Q and k = 2, this yields the Corollaire: for f in S_2(Gamma_0(N)) a rational normalized newform with associated elliptic curve E_f, the L-function of E_f equals L(f, s) and the geometric conductor of E_f equals N - the latter using Ogg's work.

**Acceptance.** Check the principal-series formula sigma(Ind(xi_1, xi_2)) = xi_1^{-1} omega_p^{-1/2} + xi_2^{-1} omega_p^{-1/2} against the determinant relation det sigma(pi_p) = chi_{pi_p}^{-1} omega_p^{-1}, and confirm the normalization is the source's, not Langlands' Check that the special (Steinberg) case gives a nonzero monodromy operator on both sides, so the comparison is not merely of semisimplifications

**Direct inputs:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/hilbert-modular-compatible-system-carayol-theorem-A`, `GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransfer:R16.3`, `ArithmeticGaloisRepresentations:R01.2`.

**Source anchors:** [Henri Carayol](https://www.numdam.org/article/ASENS_1986_4_19_3_409_0.pdf), 0.5, printed p. 410; [Henri Carayol](https://www.numdam.org/article/ASENS_1986_4_19_3_409_0.pdf), 0.5, printed p. 410; [Henri Carayol](https://www.numdam.org/article/ASENS_1986_4_19_3_409_0.pdf), 0.5(a), printed p. 410; [Henri Carayol](https://www.numdam.org/article/ASENS_1986_4_19_3_409_0.pdf), 0.8 Corollaire, printed p. 411.

#### Conductor, Steinberg local form and bad Euler factors for classical newforms

**Identifier:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.4/conductor-and-local-factors-classical`; theorem. Planet: *Conductor equals level*.

Let f be a newform of weight k ≥ 2, level N and character ψ, and ρ = ρ_{f,λ} with λ | ℓ. (a) The Artin conductor of ρ (away from ℓ) is the prime-to-ℓ part of N. (b) If p ≠ ℓ, p ∥ N and p does not divide the conductor of ψ, then ρ|_{G_p} ≅ (χ_uχ_ℓ ∗; 0 χ_u) for the unramified character χ_u with χ_u(Frob_p) = a_p (arithmetic), with ∗ ramified (N ≠ 0, Steinberg); if p ∥ N and p divides the conductor of ψ, ρ|_{G_p} ≅ χ_u^{−1}χ_ℓ^{k−1}ψ′|_{G_p} ⊕ χ_u, where ψ′ is the Galois character of ψ, with ψ′(Frob_q) = ψ(q) at arithmetic Frobenius. (c) For every p ≠ ℓ, the local Euler factor det(1 − Frob_p^{geom} p^{−s} | M^{I_p})⁻¹ of the cohomological realisation M = ρ^∨ equals the Euler factor of L(f, s) at p: (1 − a_pp^{−s} + ψ(p)p^{k−1−2s})⁻¹ for p ∤ N and (1 − a_pp^{−s})⁻¹ for p | N (on ρ^{I_p} itself, arithmetic Frobenius acts at a Steinberg place by a_p·p, not a_p). (d) For a rational weight-two newform (K_f = Q) with its elliptic curve E_f: L(E_f, s) = L(f, s) and the conductor of E_f is N.

**Hypotheses.** (a)–(c) are the F = Q case of Carayol's Theorem (A) through the Hecke-to-Langlands dictionary (AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.4/hecke-versus-langlands-normalization-and-local-global-compatibility); Darmon–Diamond–Taylor Theorem 3.1(d)–(e) state (a)–(b) in weight two. (d) is Carayol's Corollaire (0.8), using Ogg for the conductor of E_f. Full Frobenius-semisimple Weil–Deligne data including N are compared, not only semisimplified inertia (stage text).

**Proof outline.** Local–global compatibility away from ℓ (AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.4/hecke-versus-langlands-normalization-and-local-global-compatibility) identifies WD(ρ|_{G_p})^{F-ss} with the parameter of π_p(f). The conductor of π_p(f) is p^{v_p(N)} (newform theory, GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransfer R16.3 request) and local Langlands preserves conductors, giving (a). p ∥ N with ψ unramified at p: π_p(f) is an unramified twist of Steinberg, whose parameter has N ≠ 0 and the displayed shape; with ψ ramified at p: a principal series with one ramified character (Darmon–Diamond–Taylor 3.1(e)). L-factors: local Langlands preserves L-factors, so det(1 − Frob_p^{geom} X | M^{I_p}) is the Euler factor of π_p(f) (c); for the Steinberg form of (b), M^{I_p} is the line on which geometric Frobenius acts by a_p. (d): ρ_{f,ℓ} ≅ V_ℓ(E_f) by Čebotarev (AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.6/weight-two-tate-module-decomposition with K_f = Q), then (a) and Ogg's formula relate the Artin and geometric conductors.

**Acceptance.** 11a1: conductor 11, split multiplicative at 11 with a_11 = 1, local Euler factor (1 − 11^{−s})^{−1}; ρ|_{G_11} is the Tate-curve extension. 15a1: conductor 15 = 3·5; at 3 non-split (a_3 = −1), at 5 split (a_5 = 1). Non-example: matching unramified traces alone does not determine N at p ∥ N (the Steinberg extension and its split semisimplification have the same traces).

**Direct inputs:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.4/hecke-versus-langlands-normalization-and-local-global-compatibility`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/hilbert-modular-compatible-system-carayol-theorem-A`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.6/weight-two-tate-module-decomposition`, `GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransfer:R16.3`, `ArithmeticGaloisRepresentations:R01.6`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.4/all-hilbert-local-global-compatibility`.

Darmon, F. Diamond and R. Taylor](https://www.math.mcgill.ca/darmon/pub/Articles/Expository/05.DDT/paper.pdf), Theorem 3.1(e), p. 86; [Henri Carayol](https://www.numdam.org/article/ASENS_1986_4_19_3_409_0.pdf), 0.8, Corollaire, p. 411 (transcribed from the page image).

#### The Steinberg local form at the ramification places of the quaternion algebra

**Identifier:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.4/quaternionic-sigma-place-local-form`; theorem.

Setting of Khare–Wintenberger II §7: F totally real of even degree with p unramified in F, D the definite quaternion algebra over F ramified at all infinite places and at a finite set Σ of finite places (|Σ| even), U = ∏U_v an open subgroup compact modulo the centre of (D ⊗ A_F^∞)^×, S a finite set of places containing Σ, the places above p and those where U_v is not maximal, ψ a character of F^×\(A_F^∞)^× with values in O, k ≥ 2 a (parallel) weight (k = 2 when p = 2), S_{k,ψ}(U, O) the space of O-valued forms, and T_ψ(U) the O-algebra generated by the operators T_v, S_v (v ∉ S). A maximal ideal m is non-Eisenstein if it is not Eisenstein in the sense of KW II §7. Assume moreover that U_v = (O_D)_v^× for v ∈ Σ when p > 2 and U_v = D_v^× for v ∈ Σ when p = 2, that m is non-Eisenstein, and that Σ is disjoint from the places above p if k > 2. For p > 2 and v ∈ Σ not above p assume moreover that N(v) ≢ −1 mod p, or that ρ̄_m|_{D_v} is not the direct sum of two unramified characters. Then each v ∈ Σ carries one unramified character γ_v: G_{F_v} → O^×, independent of the eigenform, with γ_v² = ψ_v and ρ_f|_{D_v} ≅ (χ_pγ_v ∗; 0 γ_v) for all Hecke eigenforms f ∈ S_{k,ψ}(U, O)_m. Here ρ_f is Khare–Wintenberger's arithmetic representation, which is ρ_{π,λ}^∨ in this roadmap's normalisation (R19.2/hilbert-normalisation-dictionary (j)); for ρ_{π,λ} itself the form is (γ_v^{−1} ∗; 0 γ_v^{−1}χ_p^{−1}). The value γ_v(Frob_v) at an arithmetic Frobenius is read off the action of a uniformiser of D_v^× on f: the square of the uniformiser acts through ψ(π_v) = γ_v(Frob_v)² (U_v acting trivially on the coefficients at such v), so γ_v(Frob_v) is the eigenvalue of right translation by the uniformiser, and the inverse of the eigenvalue for the inverse action (the Remark after Lemma 7.2 speaks of the inverse). This is the fixed-character assertion for eigenform specialisations. An integral triangular family over the Hecke algebra additionally requires a finite-projective invariant line as specified in the local-condition node.

**Hypotheses.** The form (χ_pγ_{v,f} ∗; 0 γ_{v,f}) for each f comes from Jacquet–Langlands at v ∈ Σ (π_v an unramified twist of Steinberg) and Carayol's local–global compatibility at v ∤ p; at v | p, which is allowed when k = 2, it comes from the coefficient-prime theorem, which applies without a residual hypothesis because π_v is discrete series (R19.5/potential-semistability-and-compatibility-at-the-coefficient-prime). The independence of f is the new content. The final density argument concerns the reduced O-flat KW Hecke algebra. A nilpotent family requires the local-condition-quotient proof in R19.6/hecke-family-local-conditions; dense field points alone do not suffice. Use KW II §7 pp. 58–59: O is the integer ring of a sufficiently large finite E/Q_p containing all embeddings F→E; D splits after extension to E at p; in Lemma 7.2 (p. 60) and in §9.1.1, Σ∩{v|p}=∅ if k>2. The coefficient representation τ has τ|U∩centre=ψ⁻¹, with ψ restricting on an open subgroup of the p-units to the norm power 2−k. For p=2 and noncompact U, fix one of the extensions of the trivial W₂ action from its maximal compact subgroup U⁰ to U·centre. The bad set S also contains infinity and places where τ is nontrivial.

**Proof outline.** Jacquet–Langlands at v ∈ Σ sends the one-dimensional representation of D_v^× to an unramified twist of Steinberg (GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransfer R17.3, request); local–global compatibility (AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.4/hecke-versus-langlands-normalization-and-local-global-compatibility) gives ρ_f|_{D_v} ≅ (χ_pγ_{v,f} ∗; 0 γ_{v,f}) with γ_{v,f} unramified and γ_{v,f}² = ψ_v, after dualising to the arithmetic convention; for v | p use the coefficient-prime theorem instead. p > 2: γ_{v,f} is one of the two square roots γ, γη of ψ_v (η the unramified quadratic character), and the residual representation is independent of f (all f lie in the localisation at m). The semisimplified residual restriction is {χ̄_pγ̄, γ̄} or {χ̄_pγ̄η̄, γ̄η̄}; these coincide only if χ̄_p|_{D_v} = η̄, that is v ∤ p and N(v) ≡ −1 mod p, and in that case a non-split ρ̄_m|_{D_v} still determines γ̄ as its unramified quotient. Under the hypothesis of the statement γ_{v,f} is therefore independent of f. The source gives only the first sentence of this argument. p = 2: with U_v = D_v^×, the local component at v of the forms on D^× is independent of f, hence so is its Jacquet–Langlands transfer and γ_v. The fixed-character assertion is fibrewise, as in KW II Lemma 7.2. An integral family invariant line is a separate condition in R19.6/hecke-family-local-conditions and is recorded as a gap; it is not concluded from density of eigenform points.

**Acceptance.** F real quadratic, D ramified at both infinite places and at two finite places v_1, v_2: the weight-two eigenforms in a non-Eisenstein localisation all have ρ_f|_{D_{v_i}} ≅ (χ_pγ_{v_i} ∗; 0 γ_{v_i}) with the same γ_{v_i}, and γ_{v_i}(Frob_{v_i}) = ±1 when ψ = 1. Over Q the analogous statement is the Steinberg form of AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.4/conductor-and-local-factors-classical (b) (11a1 at 11: a_11 = 1).

**Direct inputs:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.4/hecke-versus-langlands-normalization-and-local-global-compatibility`, `GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransfer:R17.3`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.4/conductor-and-local-factors-classical`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.6/hecke-algebra-representation-quaternionic`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.5/potential-semistability-and-compatibility-at-the-coefficient-prime`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/hilbert-normalisation-dictionary`.

#### Local–global compatibility away from p for nearly ordinary Hilbert modular forms

**Identifier:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.4/nearly-ordinary-hilbert-compatibility-away-from-p`; theorem.

With π ∈ Π^ord_k(U) and ρ_π as in AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/wiles-ordinary-hilbert-representation, for every finite place v ∤ p, π_v ≅ π_v(ρ_π), where π_v(ρ) is the representation of GL_2(F_v) attached to ρ|_{D_v} by the rule of Skinner–Wiles (3.3): if ρ|_{D_v} ≅ (μ_1 ∗; 0 μ_2) then π_v(ρ) is a constituent of π(μ_1^{−1}|·|_v^{−1/2}, μ_2^{−1}|·|_v^{−1/2}), the characters μ_i being transported to F_v^× by the arithmetic normalisation of class field theory. The formula is that of Carayol's Hecke correspondence, but with the opposite normalisation of class field theory: in the geometric normalisation it says ρ_π|W_{F_v} ≅ Rec_v(π_v ⊗ |·|_v^{1/2}) = σ(π_v)^∨.

**Hypotheses.** For ordinary π this is Wiles [W2, Theorem 2.1.3]; the twisting argument uses the compatibility π_v ⊗ ψ_v ↔ ρ ⊗ ψ^{−1}, with ψ transported by the arithmetic normalisation; it is the dual form of AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.4/hecke-versus-langlands-normalization-and-local-global-compatibility (b). Skinner–Wiles normalise class field theory so that uniformisers correspond to arithmetic Frobenius elements (§2.1, p. 9), the opposite of Carayol's convention, and their Hecke operators act on π through π(g^{−1}) ((3.1′), p. 30). With these conventions the Frobenius elements of (3.2) are arithmetic, and (3.3) reads ρ_π|W_{F_v} ≅ Rec_v(π_v ⊗ |·|_v^{1/2}) = σ(π_v)^∨ in the geometric normalisation. So the Skinner–Wiles ρ_π is σ_λ(π)^∨ = ρ_{π,λ} ⊗ ε_ℓ in this roadmap's normalisation, not σ_λ(π) (R19.2/hilbert-normalisation-dictionary (g)); for an elliptic curve it is V_ℓ(E).

**Proof outline.** Ordinary π: [W2, Theorem 2.1.3] (quoted). Nearly ordinary π: choose ψ unramified at v with π ⊗ ψ ordinary; then π_v ⊗ ψ_v = (π ⊗ ψ)_v ≅ π_v(ρ_{π⊗ψ}) = π_v(ρ_π ⊗ ψ^{−1}) = π_v(ρ_π) ⊗ ψ_v, and cancel ψ_v (Skinner–Wiles p. 39).

**Acceptance.** A nearly ordinary π with π_v an unramified twist of Steinberg at v ∤ p has ρ_π|_{D_v} ≅ (χ_pγ ∗; 0 γ), the shape of the Tate module of a Tate curve and the dual of Carayol's special form (unramified subrepresentation), consistent with AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.4/quaternionic-sigma-place-local-form.

**Direct inputs:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/wiles-ordinary-hilbert-representation`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.4/hecke-versus-langlands-normalization-and-local-global-compatibility`, `GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransfer:R16.3`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/hilbert-normalisation-dictionary`.

**Source anchors:** [C. M. Skinner and A. J. Wiles](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1999__89__5_0.pdf), §3.3, (3.3), p. 39; [C. M. Skinner and A. J. Wiles](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1999__89__5_0.pdf), §2.1, p. 9.

#### Full local–global compatibility away from the coefficient prime

**Identifier:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.4/all-hilbert-local-global-compatibility`; theorem.

Let π be a cuspidal automorphic representation of GL₂(A_F), F totally real of degree d, of infinity type (k,w): for every real place τ, π_τ is the discrete series D_{k_τ,w} with Blattner parameter k_τ and central character t ↦ sgn(t)^{k_τ}|t|^{−w} (Carayol 0.2–0.3; Skinner §1), with k_τ ≥ 2 and k_τ ≡ w mod 2 for all τ, including d even with π principal series at every finite place. For every finite place v ∤ ℓ: WD(ρ_{π,λ}|G_{F_v})^{F-ss} ≅ ιRec_v(π_v ⊗ |·|_v^{−1/2}) as Frobenius-semisimple Weil–Deligne representations, including the monodromy operator (Skinner, equation (1)). In Carayol's normalisation this reads σ_λ(π)|W_{F_v} ≅ σ(π_v), Theorem (A) (R19.2/hilbert-normalisation-dictionary (a)). Consequently the Artin conductor of ρ_{π,λ} at v is a(π_v), L(WD(ρ_{π,λ}|G_{F_v}), s) = L(s − 1/2, π_v), and N ≠ 0 exactly when π_v is a twist of the Steinberg representation.

**Hypotheses.** Frobenius-semisimplification keeps the Jordan form of N; agreement of traces or of semisimplified inertia alone is weaker. Rec_v is normalised as in Skinner (uniformisers to geometric Frobenius), so the statement is about ρ_{π,λ}, not σ_λ(π).

**Proof outline.** Geometric case (d odd or a finite discrete-series place): Carayol's Theorem (A) (R19.2/hilbert-modular-compatible-system-carayol-theorem-A), including the extraordinary places in residue characteristic 2, which need cyclic and non-normal cubic base change (GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransfer R17.4); translate by the dictionary. Remaining case: the representation is Taylor's congruence construction, and the full compatibility at every v ∤ ℓ is the statement recalled by Skinner in (1) and by Blasius §2.3 and §3, with proofs by Taylor [Tay1, Tay2]; Taylor's original papers are a recorded source gap. Conductors and L-factors: local Langlands preserves them, and the unramified twist by |·|^{−1/2} shifts s by 1/2 (GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransfer R16.3).

**Acceptance.** The split semisimplification of a Steinberg parameter has the same Frobenius traces but the wrong N, conductor and bad factor. An even-degree field with π principal series at every finite place is covered. 11a1 at 11: conductor exponent 1 and L-factor (1 − 11^{−s})^{−1} for the cohomological normalisation.

**Direct inputs:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/all-cohomological-hilbert-representation`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/hilbert-normalisation-dictionary`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/hilbert-modular-compatible-system-carayol-theorem-A`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.4/hecke-versus-langlands-normalization-and-local-global-compatibility`, `GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransfer:R16.3`, `GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransfer:R17.4`, `ArithmeticGaloisRepresentations:R01.2`.

**Source anchors:** [Christopher Skinner](https://content.ems.press/assets/public/full-texts/serials/dm/14/8965206/online/10.4171-dm-272.pdf), Introduction, p. 242; [Don Blasius](https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0511007), §3, p. 18; [Henri Carayol](https://www.numdam.org/article/ASENS_1986_4_19_3_409_0.pdf), Theorem (A), §12.2–12.3.

### R19.5 specifications

#### Scholl–Saito comparison for geometric eigenform realisations

**Identifier:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.5/potential-semistability-and-compatibility-at-the-coefficient-prime`; theorem. Planet: *Compatibility at the coefficient prime*.

For the classical parabolic/Kuga–Sato newform realisation, and for Hilbert forms of degree g>1 and multiweight w≥k_τ≥2, k_τ≡w mod 2, satisfying Saito’s finite-discrete-series-place hypothesis when g is even, apply the imported generic proper-smooth/semistable comparison to the coefficient projector. Obtain de Rham/potentially semistable realisations, their labelled Hodge filtration and the full WD^{F-ss} comparison at the coefficient prime. In the classical good-prime case p∤N the arithmetic representation is crystalline with Hodge weights {0,k−1} in the convention HT(χ_p)=1; the cohomological dual has the opposite representation weights. Its Euler roots in the cohomological D_cris normalisation have polynomial X²−a_pX+ε(p)p^{k−1}. Weight two gives the Barsotti–Tate realisation; arbitrary k does not. This is the concrete modular comparison application owned by R19.5. In this roadmap's normalisation the Hilbert conclusion reads WD(ρ_{π,λ}|G_{F_v})^{F-ss} ≅ ιRec_v(π_v ⊗ |·|_v^{−1/2}), because Saito's σ̌_h(π_{f,p}) is Carayol's σ(π_v) = Rec_v(π_v ⊗ |·|_v^{−1/2}) ⊗ χ_{π_v}^{−1} and ρ_{f,λ} = σ_λ(π_f) (R19.2/hilbert-normalisation-dictionary (a)–(b)). The classical branch (F = Q, every k ≥ 2 and every coefficient prime) is the case d = 1 of Skinner's Theorem 1, which attributes it to Saito 1997; its p-adic weight–monodromy statement is Saito 2000, Theorem 3.

**Hypotheses.** Classical k≥2, or Hilbert cohomological multiweight with Saito’s geometry hypotheses. Retain the coefficient projector and its realisation functor laws; comparison is not deduced just from a desired Euler polynomial. The full all-Hilbert extensions are the separate Kisin and Skinner nodes. For the Hilbert branch retain all of Saito’s standing degree/multiweight hypotheses (g > 1; finite discrete-series place when g is even). The classical all-weight, all-prime theorem is anchored in Skinner's Theorem 1 with d = 1 and in Saito 2000; Saito 1997 itself is a recorded source gap.

**Proof outline.** Use the Scholl projector or Carayol multiplicity space on the imported geometry. Apply generic R06.5 comparison functorially to correspondences and coefficient projectors; identify the de Rham cusp-form/Hodge filtration. Saito compares traces on W⁺; purity distinguishes zero from nonzero monodromy to recover the full WD parameter. The statement for ρ_{π,λ} = σ_λ(π) ⊗ χ_{π,λ} follows because χ_{π,λ} is de Rham at v with Weil–Deligne representation χ_{π_v} (AutomorphicGaloisRepresentationsPartII AG2.0/galois-character-of-an-algebraic-hecke-character). At a good classical prime use proper-smooth crystalline comparison; in weight two compare the p-divisible group of R14.5’s abelian quotient.

**Acceptance.** 11a1 at 11: Steinberg, semistable with N ≠ 0 (the Tate curve). 11a1 at 3: crystalline, ordinary; at 2: crystalline, not ordinary.

#### Local behaviour at p of Hilbert modular Galois representations (Kisin, Saito)

**Identifier:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.5/hilbert-local-behaviour-at-p`; theorem.

In the unramified-base-field parallel-weight setting of KW II Lemma 7.7 and Corollary 7.8, with absolutely irreducible residual representation, use the full Kisin coefficient-prime theorem. The shapes below are those of Khare–Wintenberger's arithmetic ρ_π, which is ρ_{π,λ}^∨ for π of infinity type (k, k − 2) (R19.2/hilbert-normalisation-dictionary (j)). At v|p, an unramified π_v gives a crystalline representation with the specified weight; in weight two a tame principal-series type gives potentially Barsotti–Tate behaviour over the extension killing that type. In the weight-two unramified Steinberg-twist case the representation is semistable noncrystalline with characters χ_pγ_v and γ_v (γ_v unramified), weights {0,1}, and nonzero monodromy. For higher weights use the general Hodge type and WD parameter: neither weights {0,1} nor this ordinary triangular shape is asserted automatically. Ordinary shape is imported from R21.3 with its exact character hypotheses.

**Hypotheses.** KW’s Corollary 7.8 has F unramified at p and parallel weights; Kisin Theorem 4.3 itself has arbitrary totally real F and cohomological multiweight. Retain absolute residual irreducibility for this Kisin route; the separate Skinner theorem removes it. In the tame potentially Barsotti–Tate case the local character factors through the norm as in KW type (B); the exponent in ω_p^{k−2} is a residual Serre weight, not the characteristic-zero weight two.

**Proof outline.** Apply the full Kisin coefficient-prime node to ρ_{π,λ} and dualise (the dictionary), then generic R06.3 WD descent: unramified parameter and N=0 imply crystalline. For the tame type kill its inertia on the stated extension and use the weight-two comparison with finite-flat/Barsotti–Tate objects from R07.4. For a weight-two Steinberg twist retain N≠0; the ordinary character formula comes from the imported R21.3 theorem, not merely from the presence of monodromy.

**Acceptance.** 11a1 at 11 (F = Q, k = 2, Steinberg): case (iii), the Tate curve. 11a1 at 3: case (i), crystalline, ordinary (a_3 = −1).

**Direct inputs:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.5/kisin-hilbert-coefficient-prime`, `PadicHodgeTheory:R06.3/weil-deligne-descent`, `FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheory:R07.4`, `OrdinaryAutomorphicFormsAndModularityLifting:R21.3`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/hilbert-normalisation-dictionary`.

**Source anchors:** [C. Khare and J.-P. Wintenberger](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), Lemma 7.7, p. 67 (authors' final version); [C. Khare and J.-P. Wintenberger](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), Corollary 7.8(i), p. 67; [C. Khare and J.-P. Wintenberger](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), Corollary 7.8, Steinberg case, p. 68.

#### Kisin's coefficient-prime compatibility theorem

**Identifier:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.5/kisin-hilbert-coefficient-prime`; theorem. Planet: *Kisin compatibility theorem*.

Kisin's Theorem 4.3 in this roadmap's normalisation: let π be a cuspidal automorphic representation of GL₂(A_F), F totally real of degree d, of infinity type (k,w): for every real place τ, π_τ is the discrete series D_{k_τ,w} with Blattner parameter k_τ and central character t ↦ sgn(t)^{k_τ}|t|^{−w} (Carayol 0.2–0.3; Skinner §1), with k_τ ≥ 2 and k_τ ≡ w mod 2 for all τ, λ | p, and suppose the semisimplified reduction of ρ_{π,λ} is absolutely irreducible. Then for every place v | p of F, ρ_{π,λ}|G_{F_v} is potentially semistable with Hodge degrees (w − k_τ + 2)/2 and (w + k_τ)/2 at each τ, and the Frobenius-semisimplification of its Weil–Deligne representation (Fontaine's covariant D_pst, Kisin §4.2) is ιRec_v(π_v ⊗ |·|_v^{−1/2}), preserving N. Kisin's ρ_{π,λ} is defined by the geometric Frobenius polynomial X² − t_vX + N(v)s_v with positive-uniformiser operators, so it is ρ_{π,λ} itself, and the local Langlands correspondence of his Theorem 4.3 is the one fixed by (4.3.1), tr(w | σ_v) = t_v. No unramified-base, parallel-weight or discrete-series hypothesis is imposed. In the proof the fixed-type quotient and its period module exist over an arbitrary complete Noetherian local coefficient algebra A°.

**Hypotheses.** Residual absolute irreducibility is essential to this route; R19.5/skinner-full-hilbert-coefficient-prime removes it. The quotient property tests all finite Q_p-algebras, including algebras with nilpotents; field-valued points alone do not express it.

**Proof outline.** If π has a finite discrete-series place, apply Saito's comparison (R19.5/potential-semistability-and-compatibility-at-the-coefficient-prime) and translate by the dictionary. Otherwise Taylor's auxiliary μ-new congruences modulo p^s for every s (R19.2/all-cohomological-hilbert-representation) have characteristic-zero points special at μ, which satisfy Saito's theorem and factor through the fixed crystalline Hodge-type quotient. Apply Kisin's Theorems 2.5.5 and 2.7.6 for arbitrary A° (LocalGaloisDeformationRings R08.3): the period module D_R with φ, and the trace T_φ of φ^{ν(w)} as a function on the quotient. Congruences at every precision, closed quotient ideals and completeness give the factorisation R_{V_F} → T_m → O_{π,λ} through the quotient and T_φ ↦ t_v; Frobenius-semisimplification keeps N.

**Acceptance.** A ramified totally real base field and a non-parallel cohomological weight are allowed. A residually reducible λ is not covered by this theorem merely because ρ_{π,λ} is irreducible in characteristic zero.

**Direct inputs:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/all-cohomological-hilbert-representation`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2/hilbert-normalisation-dictionary`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.5/potential-semistability-and-compatibility-at-the-coefficient-prime`, `LocalGaloisDeformationRings:R08.3/semistable-height-quotient`, `LocalGaloisDeformationRings:R08.3`, `IntegralHeckeAndGaloisDeterminants:IHG.1`.

**Source anchors:** [Mark Kisin](https://www.ams.org/journals/jams/2008-21-02/S0894-0347-07-00576-0/S0894-0347-07-00576-0.pdf), Theorem 4.3 and proof, pp. 543–544; [Mark Kisin](https://www.ams.org/journals/jams/2008-21-02/S0894-0347-07-00576-0/S0894-0347-07-00576-0.pdf), Theorem 2.5.5, pp. 530–531; Theorem 2.7.6, p. 534; [Mark Kisin](https://www.ams.org/journals/jams/2008-21-02/S0894-0347-07-00576-0/S0894-0347-07-00576-0.pdf), §4.1, p. 542.

#### Skinner's full Hilbert coefficient-prime theorem

**Identifier:** `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.5/skinner-full-hilbert-coefficient-prime`; theorem. Planet: *Skinner's coefficient-prime theorem*.

Skinner's Theorem 1 in this roadmap's normalisation: let π be a cuspidal automorphic representation of GL₂(A_F), F totally real of degree d, of infinity type (k,w): for every real place τ, π_τ is the discrete series D_{k_τ,w} with Blattner parameter k_τ and central character t ↦ sgn(t)^{k_τ}|t|^{−w} (Carayol 0.2–0.3; Skinner §1), with k_τ ≥ 2 and k_τ ≡ w mod 2 for all τ, λ | p, and v | p. Then ρ_{π,λ}|G_{F_v} is potentially semistable with Hodge degrees (w − k_τ + 2)/2 and (w + k_τ)/2 at each τ, and WD(ρ_{π,λ}|G_{F_v})^{F-ss} ≅ ιRec_v(π_v ⊗ |·|_v^{−1/2}) (Skinner (2)). Skinner's ρ_π is ρ_{π,λ} (his equation (1) and the trace identity of §2.4.2); his printed degrees (w − k_τ)/2 and (w + k_τ − 2)/2 are these under the shift of sourceIssue E4. No residual irreducibility and no discrete-series place is assumed.

**Hypotheses.** Labelled degrees in the grading of the dictionary; the comparison is of full WD^{F-ss}, not only of Hodge numbers. Generic period rings, D_pst and the Weil–Deligne functor are PadicHodgeTheory R06.3; this arithmetic theorem is owned here.

**Proof outline.** Known cases: Saito when d is odd or π has a finite discrete-series place (R19.5/potential-semistability-and-compatibility-at-the-coefficient-prime); Blasius–Rogawski when some k_τ > 2 and π is unramified above p (Skinner §2.3.2, through AutomorphicGaloisRepresentationsPartII AG2.1 and AG2.2 and PadicHodgeTheory R06.5); CM representations by Serre. Reduction (Skinner §2.2): potential crystallinity and the trace identity (4) may be checked after an abelian base change F′/F with π_{F′} cuspidal and unramified above p (GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransfer R17.4), because ρ_{π_{F′},λ} ≅ ρ_{π,λ}|G_{F′} (R19.2/hilbert-uniqueness-determinant-oddness-irreducibility); so assume d even, k_τ = 2, π principal series everywhere, unramified above p, not CM, and twist to w = 0. Symmetric square (Skinner §2.4.1): by R19.2/hilbert-hodge-tate-property, ρ_v := ρ_{π,λ}|G_{F_v} is a quadratic twist of a crystalline representation, and the GL₃ realisation gives WD(Sym²ρ_v) ≅ ιRec_v(Sym²π_v ⊗ |·|_v^{−1}); hence WD(ρ_v) is a quadratic twist of ιRec_v(π_v ⊗ |·|_v^{−1/2}), and it suffices to show D_cris(ρ_v)^{φ^{f_v} = q_v^{1/2}α_v(ϖ_v)} ≠ 0 for π_v = π(α_v, β_v). Family (Skinner §2.4.2): with D the totally definite quaternion algebra over F and Buzzard's overconvergent forms S^D_κ(U; m) over a weight disc B_r, a T^S-stable finite slope summand N gives a reduced affinoid A, φ: T^S → A and ρ_A on M = A ⊕ C (PadicFamilies L2a and L2, with Jacquet–Langlands GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransfer R17.3); the specialisations at the dense set of classical points x, of infinity type ((n_x + 2)_τ, n_x) in this roadmap's normalisation (the source writes ((n_x + 2), n_x + 2), source issue E4), are ρ_{π_x,λ}, using their irreducibility (R19.2/hilbert-uniqueness-determinant-oddness-irreducibility). Analytic continuation: Theorem 1 holds at those points by the Blasius–Rogawski case, so D_cris(ρ_x|D_v)^{φ^{f_v} = x(φ_v)} has rank ≥ 1; Kisin's analytic continuation of crystalline periods [Ki2, (5.14)–(5.15)], extended from G_{Q_p} to G_{F_v} as in Skinner's footnote 9 (PadicHodgeTheory R06.3), gives the same at x₀, which removes the quadratic ambiguity.

**Acceptance.** The even-degree parallel-weight-two case with reducible residual representation is included. The assertion compares full WD^{F-ss}, not only the two labelled Hodge degrees. 11a1 at 11 (F = Q, d = 1 odd, Saito's case): semistable non-crystalline with N ≠ 0.
