- **`ModularCurvesPartII:R14.2`:** The weight-two Hecke algebra 𝕋_ℤ of Γ_H(N) generated by the T_n and ⟨d⟩, acting on J_Γ and T_ℓ(J_Γ) compatibly with G_ℚ; faithfulness on S₂(Γ, R) and finite freeness of 𝕋_R; the Weil-pairing adjointness of T and w_N T w_N; and H₁(X_Γ, ℤ) ⊗ ℚ free of rank two over 𝕋_ℚ (Darmon–Diamond–Taylor Lemmas 1.34–1.39). Consumers: `R19.6/full-weight-two-hecke-algebra-and-its-galois-representations`, `R19.6/weight-two-tate-module-decomposition`.

- **`GlobalGaloisDeformations:R04.3`:** The universal deformation ring R_Σ of liftings of type Σ of an odd absolutely irreducible ρ̄ : G_ℚ → GL₂(k), ℓ odd (determinant ε, the flat, ordinary or semistable condition at ℓ, minimal ramification at p ∉ Σ, no condition at p ∈ Σ − {ℓ}), with its universal representation ρ^univ_Σ, and the stability of these local conditions under products, subobjects and quotients. Additional interface: Representability and coefficient-quotient stability of the specified fixed-determinant/minimal/ordinary/flat type-Σ local functors and their global universal ring; supply the DDT Lemma 3.27 finite-quotient argument without a false product-embedding assumption. Additional interface: For the Hilbert non-Eisenstein residual representation, the universal fixed determinant ψχ_p deformation functor unramified outside S (and with every local condition explicitly named when one is imposed). Check the Schur and finite-generation hypotheses of R04.2 before invoking the universal ring. Consumers: `R19.6/hecke-algebra-representation-classical`, `R19.6/hecke-family-local-conditions`, `R19.6/hecke-algebra-representation-quaternionic`.

- **`SerreWeightAndLevelOptimisation:R20.6`:** Darmon–Diamond–Taylor Theorem 3.15 (Diamond's weight-two level optimisation): for ℓ odd and ρ̄ absolutely irreducible and modular (with ρ̄|_{G_{ℚ(√−3)}} absolutely irreducible if ℓ = 3), a weight-two newform f with ρ̄_f ≅ ρ̄, N_f = N(ρ̄)ℓ^{δ(ρ̄)} and ψ_f of order prime to ℓ. Additional interface: Endpoint residual weight p+1 versus existence of a weight-two potentially Barsotti–Tate/ordinary lift, with every irreducibility and local-type hypothesis. Do not infer this from crystalline weight p+1 alone. Consumers: `R19.6/hecke-algebra-representation-classical`, `R19.5/endpoint-weight-local-contract`.

- **`AlgebraicModularFormsAndSerreWeights:R15.2`:** Generation of the weight-two Hecke algebra 𝕋_R of Γ_H(N) (Darmon–Diamond–Taylor Lemma 4.1): by the T_n, or by the T_p and ⟨d⟩; and, for D prime to N with D odd or 2 invertible in R, by the T_n with (n, D) = 1 (Diamond–Im Proposition 3.5.1; Wiles 1995, p. 491). Consumers: `R19.6/reduced-hecke-algebra-as-a-localisation`, `R19.6/full-weight-two-hecke-algebra-and-its-galois-representations`.

- **`tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/ModularForms#layer-4-eigenforms-newforms-primitive-forms-the-conductor`:** The newform decomposition S₂(Γ_H(N), K) = ⊕_f S_{K,f} over newforms f of level N_f | N, with S_{K,f} spanned by the f(aτ), a | N/N_f (Darmon–Diamond–Taylor Theorem 1.22), and the characteristic polynomial u^{v−1}(u² − a_p(f)u + ψ_f(p)p) of T_p on the span of the f(ap^iτ), v = v_p(N/N_f). For Deligne–Serre 5.2, in any weight k: an eigenform of the T_p, p ∤ N, has the eigenvalues of a newform of level dividing N (Atkin–Lehner–Li), whose coefficients are multiplicative with a_{p^m} = a_p^m and |a_p| < p^{k/2} at p | N. Consumers: `R19.6/reduced-hecke-algebra-as-a-localisation`, `R19.1/rankin-bound-for-a-cuspidal-eigenform`, `R19.1/lambda-adic-representation-of-a-weight-k-eigenform`.

- **`HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves:R18.5`:** The supersingular locus of Carayol's Shimura curves at 𝔭 ≠ v through Drinfeld's formal 𝒪_𝔭-modules of height 2 with level structure (Carayol §§7–9: the formalism, the universal deformation with level structure, and the supersingular orbit revisited), and Brylinski's appendix identifying the vanishing cycles with the cohomology of the generic fibre of the completed strict henselisation. These give the local fundamental representation 𝒰 of W(F̄_𝔭/F_𝔭) × GL₂(F_𝔭) × B̄_𝔭^× (10.3). Consumers: `R19.2/carayol-local-fundamental-representation`.

- **`LefschetzPencilsAndVanishingCycles:LPV.0`:** The vanishing-cycle exact sequence for a proper curve over a strictly henselian discrete valuation ring with a lisse coefficient sheaf, and its compatibility with finite étale covers and group actions, as Carayol uses it in 4.2–4.5. Consumers: `R19.2/carayol-vanishing-cycle-filtration`.

- **`LefschetzPencilsAndVanishingCycles:LPV.7:semistable-curves`:** Picard–Lefschetz for a stable curve X over a strictly henselian discrete valuation ring (after the Deligne–Mumford semistable reduction theorem): the monodromy logarithm N maps H¹(X_η̄, F)(1) onto ker(H¹(X_s, F) → H¹(X̃_s, F)), the kernel of pull-back to the normalisation X̃_s, for a lisse F (Carayol 11.4–11.10, Langlands [L.1] for F = ℚ). Consumers: `R19.2/carayol-special-places`.

- **`GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransfer:R17.4`:** Local and global base change for GL₂ along extensions of degree at most 3: Langlands for cyclic extensions and Jacquet–Piatetski-Shapiro–Shalika for non-Galois cubic ones, with the compatibility of the lift with restriction of the Weil–Deligne representation for principal series, special and ordinary cuspidal π, which Carayol (12.2.2) asserts without a reference. Additional interface: Cyclic totally real base change, descent and compatibility of attached good-prime polynomials with restriction; do not infer non-normal cubic base change from cyclic base change. Additional interface: Cyclic and non-normal cubic base change with local parameters and compatibility with Galois restriction, including Carayol §12.2.2; cyclic base change alone does not supply the cubic step. Additional interface: The existing nonnormal-cubic-base-change node supplies only almost-all Satake compatibility. Carayol §12.2.1–12.2.2 requires the stronger local parameter/restriction assertion for principal series, special and ordinary cuspidal components at the chosen place; retain this as an additional request/gap, not an implication of weak transfer. Additional interface: Abelian (cyclic, solvable) base change of a cuspidal π of infinity type (k,w) to a totally real F′, cuspidal unless π is induced from F′, compatible with Satake parameters at unramified places and with Rec at every place; totally real quadratic base change; and the Gelbart–Jacquet lift Sym²π ⊗ |·|^{−1} to GL₃, cuspidal for non-CM π. Consumers: `R19.2/carayol-cubic-base-change-of-extraordinary`, `R19.2/hilbert-modular-compatible-system-carayol-theorem-A`, `R19.2/hilbert-uniqueness-determinant-oddness-irreducibility`, `R19.4/all-hilbert-local-global-compatibility`, `R19.2/hilbert-hodge-tate-property`, `R19.3/hilbert-ramanujan-conjecture`, `R19.5/skinner-full-hilbert-coefficient-prime`.

- **`GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransfer:R17.5`:** Automorphic induction 𝒲(L, ξ) from a CM quadratic extension L/F with prescribed local component, archimedean types and behaviour at v (Carayol 11.2); Tunnell's globalisation of primitive local representations ([Tu. 1], Theorem 1.3); and the Artin conjecture for tetrahedral (Langlands) and octahedral (Tunnell) representations, with Jacquet–Langlands chapter 12. Additional interface: Algebraic Hecke-character automorphic induction for quadratic extensions, theta-series correspondence, nontrivial quadratic self-twist criterion and index-two induced representation dictionary. Additional interface: The Grunwald–Wang existence theorem in the form Blasius §2.2 uses: for a totally real F, a finite place v and a finite-order character ψ of F_v^×, a finite-order idele class character η of F with η_v = ψ. The node R17.5/finite-hecke-extension treats the related extension problem for characters of roots of unity. Consumers: `R19.2/carayol-ordinary-cuspidal-places`, `R19.2/carayol-cubic-base-change-of-extraordinary`, `R19.2/cm-hilbert-eigenform`, `R19.2/ordinary-cm-primes-split`, `R19.2/virtual-reducibility-implies-cm`, `R19.3/hilbert-ramanujan-conjecture`.

- **`ArithmeticGaloisRepresentations:R01.4`:** Dickson's classification for subgroups G of GL₂(F_ℓ) whose identity representation is semisimple, in the form of Serre, Propriétés galoisiennes des points d'ordre fini des courbes elliptiques (Invent. Math. 15, 1972), §2, Propositions 15–16, as Deligne–Serre 7.2 use it: G contains SL₂(F_ℓ), or lies in a Cartan subgroup, or lies in the normaliser of a Cartan subgroup but not in it, or has projective image A₄, S₄ or A₅. The reducible (diagonalisable) case is included. Additional interface: Continuous finite-dimensional Clifford theory and Ribet 1975 Theorem 2.3 for compact semisimple λ-adic groups with an open abelian subgroup and infinite projective image. Additional interface: Dickson classification with subfield form, exceptional-group bounds and tame-inertia order argument; generic finite/Lie-group input only, the non-CM Hilbert application is owned here. Additional interface: Generic compact λ-adic Lie-group Zariski closure, quaternionic norm-one groups, finite-group classification and integral commutator lifting. The modular inner-twist/Momose application remains owned by R19.3. Consumers: `R19.1/bounded-semisimple-subgroups-of-gl2`, `R19.2/virtual-reducibility-implies-cm`, `R19.3/dimitrov-large-image`, `R19.3/ribet-momose-classical-large-image`.

- **`ArithmeticGaloisRepresentations:R01.5`:** Čebotarev recognition in characteristic zero for representations of G_ℚ with finite image (Deligne–Serre Lemme 3.2), after realising both over a common field; coefficient descent over a finite field (Lemme 6.13); and the fact that every element of a finite image of G_ℚ is a Frobenius at a prime outside any given finite set. Additional interface: Čebotarev/Brauer–Nesbitt recognition and continuous rank-two pseudorepresentation recognition; generic character recognition only; Hilbert irreducibility is proved by the arithmetic-properties node. Additional interface: Serre §7.2 Corollary 2 trace-zero density theorem for a non-CM modular λ-adic representation, or the general p-adic analytic Chebotarev/Haar-zero hypersurface theorem that implies it. Additional interface: Continuous semisimple recognition by good Frobenius traces. Consumers: `R19.1/mod-lambda-representation-of-a-mod-lambda-eigenform`, `R19.1/weight-one-characteristic-zero-lift`, `R19.1/weight-one-artin-representation`, `R19.2/all-cohomological-hilbert-representation`, `R19.3/skinner-density-one-ordinary-primes`, `R19.2/hilbert-uniqueness-determinant-oddness-irreducibility`, `R19.2/cm-hilbert-eigenform`, `R19.6/geometric-hecke-determinant`, `R19.6/residual-representation-of-a-newform`.

- **`AutomorphicLFunctionsAndLocalFactors:AL.3`:** Rankin's theorem for a classical newform f = Σa_nqⁿ of type (k, ε) on Γ₀(N) (Rankin 1939; Deligne–Serre 5.2 also cite Jacquet LN 278, Li and Ogg): Σ|a_n|²n^{−s} converges for Re s > k, and ζ(2s − 2k + 2)·Σ|a_n|²n^{−s} extends meromorphically to ℂ with a single pole, simple, at s = k. This is the classical specialisation of the GL₂ × GL₂ Rankin–Selberg integral. Additional interface: Holomorphy of the standard L-function of a cuspidal GL₂ representation under the stated twist, and nonvanishing of the finite-order Hecke-character L-value at the noncancellation point in Skinner p. 256. Additional interface: For cuspidal π on GL₂(A_F) and an algebraic Hecke character ψ, L(π ⊗ ψ, s) is entire; Hecke L-functions L(ψ, s) do not vanish at s = 1 + n, n ≥ 0, and ζ_F has a pole at 1. Consumers: `R19.1/rankin-bound-for-a-cuspidal-eigenform`, `R19.2/hilbert-uniqueness-determinant-oddness-irreducibility`.

- **`tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/Chebotarev#layer-10-dirichlet-density-chebotarev`:** Čebotarev's density theorem in Dirichlet density over ℚ: for a finite Galois extension M/ℚ and a conjugacy class C of Gal(M/ℚ), the unramified primes with Frobenius in C have Dirichlet density |C|/[M : ℚ]; in particular the primes that split completely in a Galois number field form an infinite set. Consumers: `R19.1/uniformly-bounded-residual-images-in-weight-one`, `R19.1/mod-lambda-representation-of-a-mod-lambda-eigenform`, `R19.1/weight-one-characteristic-zero-lift`.

- **`tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/Chebotarev#layer-3-prime-sums-and-density-normalization`:** The upper Dirichlet density dens.sup X = limsup_{s→1⁺} Σ_{p∈X} p^{−s}/log(1/(s − 1)) of a set X of rational primes (Deligne–Serre (5.4.1)), with Σ_p p^{−s} = log(1/(s − 1)) + O(1), monotonicity and finite subadditivity. Consumers: `R19.1/weight-one-eigenvalues-outside-a-sparse-set`, `R19.1/uniformly-bounded-residual-images-in-weight-one`, `R19.1/weight-one-cuspidal-irreducibility`.

- **`tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/ModularForms#layer-0-diamond-operators-and-modular-forms-with-character-nebentypus`:** Weight-one Eisenstein series with character and their Hecke eigenvalues: an Eisenstein eigenform of type (1, ε) on Γ₀(N) has a_p = χ₁(p) + χ₂(p) for p ∤ N, with Dirichlet characters satisfying χ₁χ₂ = ε (Hecke, Mathematische Werke p. 690, as cited in Deligne–Serre 8.1); and the Hecke-stable decomposition M₁(N, ε) = S₁(N, ε) ⊕ E₁(N, ε). Additional interface: For weights k≥2, the Hecke-stable cusp/Eisenstein decomposition and Eisenstein good-prime eigenvalue χ₁(q)+χ₂(q)q^{k−1}, with χ₁χ₂=ε, conductor and parity hypotheses; these give the two arithmetic character summands in Deligne–Serre Theorem 6.1. Consumers: `R19.1/weight-one-artin-representation`, `R19.1/lambda-adic-representation-of-a-weight-k-eigenform`.

- **`ModularCurvesPartII:R14.6`:** Weight-two special-fibre Eichler–Shimura correspondence with Frobenius/Verschiebung components and the corrected ordinary versus supersingular subgroup-scheme fibres. Additional interface: The conventions (the model of X₁(N), the Picard or Albanese action of T_p and ⟨p⟩) under which an arithmetic Frobenius on V_ℓ(A_f) has characteristic polynomial X² − a_pX + ψ(p)p (Darmon–Diamond–Taylor Theorems 1.41 and 3.1(a)); a character of order at least three distinguishes ψ from its inverse. Consumers: `R19.1/geometric-construction-and-the-eichler-congruence-relation`, `R19.6/weight-two-tate-module-decomposition`.

- **`FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheory:R07.3`:** Integral Fontaine–Laffaille comparison with coefficients and kernels for weights [0,k−1] inside [0,ℓ−2]; do not extend the proof to endpoint weights. Additional interface: Residual labelled tame-inertia characters for the regular Hilbert weights at sufficiently large unramified coefficient characteristic; inputs to Dimitrov §§2.12–2.13 and 3.1–3.2. Additional interface: The residual representation of a classical newform on inertia at ℓ for ℓ larger than the weight and prime to the level (fundamental characters of level one or two), as Ribet 1985 Theorem 2.1 uses it. Consumers: `R19.1/integral-structure-of-the-newform-premotive`, `R19.3/dimitrov-large-image`, `R19.3/ribet-momose-classical-large-image`.

- **`OrdinaryAutomorphicFormsAndModularityLifting:R21.3`:** Wiles/Skinner–Wiles ordinary local shape (3.2), with exact subcharacter, unramified quotient, nebentypus/cyclotomic powers and hypotheses; imported by the attached-representation identification and the CM ordinary-line theorem. Additional interface: Ordinary good-prime filtration, unit-root quotient and exact graded characters; Hilbert version with labelled weights, nebentypus and specified cyclotomic powers. This roadmap owns the intersection with its fixed lattice and refinement comparison. Additional interface: The specified invariant ordinary line V_v,f with distinct graded characters in Dasgupta–Kakde §9.1; their exact ramified/unramified character hypotheses are needed. Additional interface: The exact ordinary inertia characters in KW II Corollary 7.8(i),(ii), under its residual irreducibility, unramified-base and norm-factor hypotheses. Consumers: `R19.2/wiles-ordinary-hilbert-representation`, `R19.5/ordinary-refinement-and-saturated-lattice`, `R19.2/ordinary-cm-primes-split`, `R19.2/cm-ordinary-line-complex-conjugation`, `R19.5/hilbert-local-behaviour-at-p`.

- **`GeneralizedHeegnerCycles:GH.0`:** Fine-level Kuga–Sato fibre powers, canonical equivariant smooth compactification/resolution and the boundary cohomology needed in Scholl §1.3; the ε-projector and its arithmetic application are owned here. Consumers: `R19.1/scholl-projector`, `R19.1/parabolic-realisation-premotive`.

- **`LocalGaloisDeformationRings:R08.3`:** Kisin 2.7.6/2.7.7 fixed potentially semistable/crystalline inertial and Hodge-type quotient for any complete Noetherian local A° with a free continuous family, with finite-projective period module and coefficient specialisation. The universal pst-deformation-ring node alone is insufficient. Consumers: `R19.5/kisin-hilbert-coefficient-prime`, `R19.6/hecke-family-local-conditions`.

Additional interface: Kisin's analytic continuation of crystalline periods in families (Kisin, Overconvergent modular forms and the Fontaine–Mazur conjecture, Invent. Math. 153 (2003), Proposition 5.14 and Corollary 5.15), for representations of G_{F_v} with φ replaced by φ^{f_v}, under the Sen-polynomial condition P(X) = XQ(X) with Q(0) not a zero divisor (Skinner footnote 9). Consumers: `R19.5/skinner-full-hilbert-coefficient-prime`, `R19.5/hilbert-local-behaviour-at-p`.

- **`PadicHodgeTheory:R06.5`:** Generic proper smooth/semistable geometric comparisons, projectors, cohomological pull–push and coefficient change used by Scholl/Saito/Skinner; concrete eigenform applications stay in R19.5. Additional interface: Generic semistable/proper-smooth comparison compatible with finite-group and Hecke projectors; sufficient logarithmic compactification and coefficient-functor compatibility for Scholl/Saito realisations, not a theorem already asserting the modular conclusion. Additional interface: Faltings' de Rham and Hodge–Tate comparison for smooth projective varieties over p-adic fields, and the crystalline comparison at good reduction with Katz–Messing characteristic polynomials, applied to Shimura-curve, Kuga–Sato and Picard-modular-surface families. Consumers: `R19.5/skinner-full-hilbert-coefficient-prime`, `R19.5/potential-semistability-and-compatibility-at-the-coefficient-prime`, `R19.2/hilbert-hodge-tate-property`, `R19.3/classical-newform-irreducibility`, `R19.5/shimura-curve-hk-dR-multiplicity`.

Additional interface: The precise inertial/Hodge-type characterisation of weight-two potentially Barsotti–Tate lifts, including p=2 caveats; no extension of small-range Fontaine–Laffaille to weight p+1. Additional interface: The weight-two potentially Barsotti–Tate identification used in KW II Corollary 7.8(ii), with its norm-factor and local type hypotheses. Consumers: `R19.5/potential-semistability-and-compatibility-at-the-coefficient-prime`, `R19.5/endpoint-weight-local-contract`, `R19.5/hilbert-local-behaviour-at-p`.

Repeated-root diagonalisation is not asserted. Consumers: `R19.5/good-nonordinary-crystalline-wach-realisation`.

Consumers: `R19.5/shimura-curve-hk-dR-multiplicity`, `R19.3/skinner-density-one-ordinary-primes`.

- **`SerreWeightAndLevelOptimisation:R20.2`:** Ribet level lowering at q|N for the weight-two squarefree-level trivial-character form when its absolutely irreducible residual representation is unramified at q, for sufficiently large coefficient characteristic. Consumers: `R19.3/skinner-density-one-ordinary-primes`.

- **`IntegralHeckeAndGaloisDeterminants:IHG.4`:** Integral descent/interpolation of a degree-two determinant from an actual rank-two generic Hecke module over a finite flat complete coefficient algebra: closed integral traces, cross coefficients without 1/2, universal polynomial-law identities, finite quotients and continuity. Field-valued reduced points alone are insufficient. Additional interface: For the reduced O-flat KW algebra, descend the full determinant law from its actual product representation using integral traces/determinants and mixed coefficients; record continuity. This is an instance of generic interpolation, not Theorem B over an algebraic closure. Consumers: `R19.6/geometric-hecke-determinant`, `R19.6/hecke-algebra-representation-quaternionic`.

- **`tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/ModularForms#layer-8-modular-symbols-the-integral-hecke-algebra-and-coefficient-fields`:** Use the existing modular-symbol rational structure, coefficient field, integral new Hecke algebra and ± period lines. Do not reconstruct or renormalise these upstream objects. For the weight-one sparse-eigenvalue proof, use the already specified sublayer8W inside this layer: Deligne–Serre Proposition2.7 integral-q-expansions-at-all-cusps finite-free Hecke-stable lattice, number field and algebraic-integral good-prime eigenvalues. This route precedes the Artin construction and is separate from the k≥2 modular-symbol proof; do not replan it in R15.2. Consumers: `R19.1/newform-projector-and-coefficient-descent`, `R19.1/weight-one-eigenvalues-outside-a-sparse-set`.

- **`tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/ArithmeticDirichletSeries#layer-3-local-factors-and-euler-products`:** For Dirichlet characters χ, expand log L(χ,s) in the absolutely convergent Euler product for real s>1, and bound the prime-power terms with exponent m≥2 uniformly as s→1+. Combined with nonvanishing L(χ,1), this gives bounded nontrivial-character prime sums; with the zeta residue it gives the trivial-character prime-sum logarithm used in Deligne–Serre 8.4. Consumers: `R19.1/weight-one-cuspidal-irreducibility`.

- **`tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/ModularForms#layer-8g-galois-stability-the-character-field-and-rationality`:** Use the existing conjugate-eigensystem construction over coefficient-field embeddings to obtain a weight-one eigenform with every good-prime eigenvalue σ(a_p). Layer8G imports the weight-one lattice from sublayer8W; use that branch and the existing eigenform/newform reduction when needed. Do not apply a discontinuous complex automorphism termwise to analytic functions or replan the upstream construction. Consumers: `R19.1/weight-one-eigenvalues-outside-a-sparse-set`.

- **`GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransfer:R16.2`:** Classification of the irreducible unitary generic representations of GL₂(F_v): tempered (supercuspidal, twist of Steinberg, unitary principal series) or complementary series π(μψ, μ^{−1}ψ) with μ = |·|^t, 0 < t < 1/2 (Blasius §2.2). Consumers: `R19.3/hilbert-ramanujan-conjecture`.

- **`WeightsInEtaleCohomology:R34.4`:** The weight–monodromy conjecture for H² of smooth projective surfaces over p-adic fields (Rapoport–Zink Satz 2.13 with de Jong's alterations), and de Jong's integrality: the Frobenius eigenvalues on H^j of a smooth projective variety over a local field are integral Weil numbers of integral weight (Blasius Proposition 2). Consumers: `R19.3/hilbert-ramanujan-conjecture`.

- **`WeightsInEtaleCohomology:R34.5`:** Deligne's Weil I purity for smooth projective varieties with good reduction, applied to the Shimura-curve, Kuga–Sato and unitary realisations at unramified places. Consumers: `R19.3/hilbert-ramanujan-conjecture`.

- **`PadicHodgeTheory:R06.2`:** Wintenberger's lifting of crystalline representations along central isogenies (Wintenberger, Invent. Math. 120 (1995), Theorem 1.1.3, Proposition 1.2, Remarks 1.1.4; Ann. Inst. Fourier 47 (1997), Theorem 2.2.2) for GL₂ → GL₂/{±1}: a crystalline representation of G_{F_v} into (GL₂/{±1})(Q̄_p) whose Hodge cocharacter lifts is the image of a crystalline GL₂-representation. Consumers: `R19.2/hilbert-hodge-tate-property`.

- **`AutomorphicGaloisRepresentationsPartII:AG2.1`:** The geometric realisations used for Hilbert forms: (i) Blasius–Rogawski motives, in the cohomology of the PEL unitary Shimura varieties of Skinner §2.3.1 (GU(Φ), signature (2,1) at one place and (3,0) elsewhere) with good reduction at hyperspecial level above p, for a CM base-change twist of π; (ii) the three-dimensional ρ_Π of an essentially self-dual regular cuspidal Π on GL₃(A_F) with archimedean parameters as in Skinner (8), in an abelian scheme over a Picard modular surface, with WD(ρ_Π|D_v) ≅ Rec_v(Π_v) at good places (Skinner §2.3.3, after Blasius [B1, 4.1–4.6]). Only these early realisation exports are imported; the late rank-two comparisons AG2.5/late-gl2-modular-comparison and AG2.6/rank-two-comparison-with-r19, which consume R19, are not. Consumers: `R19.2/hilbert-hodge-tate-property`, `R19.3/hilbert-ramanujan-conjecture`, `R19.5/skinner-full-hilbert-coefficient-prime`.

- **`AutomorphicGaloisRepresentationsPartII:AG2.2`:** Unitary descent of the GL₂ and GL₃ parameters used by Skinner §2.3: the L-packet τ on U(2) with non-standard base change π_E ⊗ η|·|^{1/2} ([BR, Prop. 4.1.2]), the endoscopic packet on U(Φ) with d(σ_f) = 2 ([BR, Lem. 4.2.1]), and the GL₃ packet of Π = Sym²π ⊗ |·|^{−1}. Consumers: `R19.2/hilbert-hodge-tate-property`, `R19.5/skinner-full-hilbert-coefficient-prime`.

- **`PadicFamilies:L2a`:** Group-independent eigenvariety gluing in Buzzard's form: Fredholm series of a compact U_p on orthonormalisable Banach modules over an affinoid weight disc, finite slope summands, and reduced affinoid pieces whose classical points are Zariski dense. Consumers: `R19.5/skinner-full-hilbert-coefficient-prime`.

- **`PadicFamilies:L2`:** The instance for the totally definite quaternion algebra over a totally real F (Buzzard, Eigenvarieties §§8–11; Buzzard 2004 §7): overconvergent forms S^D_κ(U; m) over a weight disc with the action of T^S ⊗ U_p and classicality for slope small relative to the weight, as used in Skinner §2.4.2. L2 states the eigencurve over Q; the quaternionic instance over F is not yet stated by any layer. Consumers: `R19.5/skinner-full-hilbert-coefficient-prime`.

## Mistakes in the sources

- **AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations/E1** (error, Pierre Deligne, Proposition 3.15 (p. 156), in its proof on p. 157; checked on the page image). Printed: For a geometric point s of M_n, the source identifies q_1^{-1}(s) with the order-p subgroups of E_s. It counts p + 1 of them when car(k(s)) ≠ p, but just one, namely the Frobenius kernel, when car(k(s)) = p. Correction: In characteristic p the fibre has one point (the kernel of Frobenius) if E_s is supersingular and two points (the kernel of Frobenius and the étale subgroup of order p) if E_s is ordinary. Reason: For ordinary E_s over an algebraically closed field of characteristic p, E_s[p] ≅ μ_p × Z/p has exactly two subgroup schemes of order p. Deligne's own Proposition 4.3 (p. 162) says that for a p-isogeny between curves with invertible Hasse invariant either its kernel or the kernel of its dual is étale, and the special fibre of M_{n,p} has two components (F and V). The proposition itself (representability and properness) and the degree p + 1 away from p are not affected. Effect: the proof. Known correction: new: no published correction found.

- **AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations/E2** (misprint, C. Khare and J.-P. Wintenberger, References, [53], p. 97 (authors' final version, 30 May 2009)). Printed: Entry [53] credits Takeshi Saito with a preprint entitled Modular forms and p-adic Hodge theory, identified by math.AG/0612077. Correction: [53] Takeshi Saito. Hilbert modular forms and p-adic Hodge theory. Preprint arXiv:math/0612077 (math.NT); Compositio Math. 145 (2009), 1081–1113. Reason: arXiv:math/0612077 is titled "Hilbert modular forms and p-adic Hodge theory" and is listed under math.NT; "Modular forms and p-adic Hodge theory" is Saito's 1997 Inventiones paper on elliptic modular forms. Lemma 7.7 concerns Hilbert modular forms, so the preprint is the intended reference. Effect: nothing. Known correction: new.

- **AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations/E3** (misprint, F. Diamond, M. Flach and L. Guo, Theorem 2.4, p. 24, and §3.3, p. 30 (arXiv v2); checked on the page images). Printed: Theorem 2.4 on p. 24 chooses integers satisfying N ≥ 3 and k ≥ 2, then defines S_N = {ℓ ∤ Nk!}. On p. 30 the category is PM^{S′}_ℚ, using S′ = { p | p ∤ N′k! }. Correction: S_N = {ℓ : ℓ | Nk!} and S′ = {p : p | N′k!}, the sets of excluded primes. Reason: In PM^S the set S is where no crystalline realisation or integral comparison is required (§1.2, p. 9), and the comparison isomorphisms are established exactly for ℓ ∤ Nk! (p. 30 and Proposition 5.6). The source itself defines S = S_N by the primes dividing Nk! (p. 13) and S′ by the primes dividing N′k! (p. 27); the printed sets are their complements. Effect: nothing. Known correction: new: present in arXiv v2 (11 Dec 2025), the latest version; the Ann. Sci. ÉNS 2004 text is organised differently and has no Theorem 2.4.

- **AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations/E4** (misprint, Christopher Skinner, §1, p. 241 (definition of infinity type (k, w)) against Theorem 1, p. 242 (Hodge–Tate type) and §2.4.1, p. 251; published Documenta text). Printed: The source specifies x ↦ sgn(x)^{k_i}|x|^{−w} for the central character on p. 241. On p. 242 it places the nonzero degrees at (w − k_{i(j)})/2 as well as (w + k_{i(j)} − 2)/2. In §2.4 it takes w = 2, with Rec_i(π_i)|C^× ≅ (z̄/z)^{1/2} ⊕ (z/z̄)^{1/2}. Correction: With the central-character convention of §1, the representation of equation (1) has D_HT non-zero in degrees (w − k_i + 2)/2 and (w + k_i)/2. The printed degrees are right when π_i has central character sgn(x)^{k_i}|x|^{2−w}, which is how §2.4 uses w (its 'w = 2' has trivial central character on R_{>0}). Reason: Skinner's own dictionary in §2.3.3 sends z^a z̄^b in Rec_i to D_HT-degree −a (checked on GL₁: |·|^{−1} ↦ (zz̄)^{−1} ↦ degree 1, the inverse cyclotomic character). For π_i with Blattner parameter k and central character sgn^k|x|^{−w}, Rec_i(π_i ⊗ |·|^{−1/2})|C^× = z^{−(w+k)/2} z̄^{−(w−k+2)/2} ⊕ (conjugate), giving degrees (w+k)/2 and (w−k+2)/2. Test: for the unitary π_E of an elliptic curve (k = 2, trivial central character, so w = 0), (1) and the trace identity T_x(frob_w) = x∘φ(char(GL₂(O_w)diag(ϖ_w,1)GL₂(O_w))) of §2.4.2 give H¹_ét(E_Q̄, Q_p), with degrees 0 and 1, while the printed formula gives −1 and 0. Effect: nothing. Known correction: new.

- **AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations/E5** (misprint, Christopher Skinner, Remark at the end of §2.4.2, p. 256; published Documenta text). Printed: From the identity L(π ⊗ ψ₂^{−1}, s − 1/2) = L(ψ₁/ψ₂, s)ζ_F(s) and L(ψ₁/ψ₂, 1) ≠ 0 the Remark concludes that L(π ⊗ ψ₂^{−1}, s) has a pole at s = 1. Correction: The pole is that of L(π ⊗ ψ₂^{−1}, s − 1/2) at s = 1, that is, of L(π ⊗ ψ₂^{−1}, s) at s = 1/2. Reason: ζ_F has its pole at s = 1 and L(ψ₁/ψ₂, 1) ≠ 0, so the right-hand side, hence L(π ⊗ ψ₂^{−1}, s − 1/2), has a pole at s = 1; substituting s + 1/2 for s places the pole of L(π ⊗ ψ₂^{−1}, s) at 1/2. For an elliptic curve E and trivial ψ₂ the left side is L(E, s) in the motivic variable. The contradiction with cuspidality, an entire L-function, is unaffected. Effect: nothing. Known correction: new.

- **AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations/E6** (gap, C. Khare and J.-P. Wintenberger, Lemma 7.2 and its proof, case (i), pp. 60–61 (authors' final version, 30 May 2009; the published Invent. Math. 178 text was not seen)). Printed: For p > 2 the proof deduces that the unramified character γ_{v,f}, a square root of ψ_v with ρ_f|D_v of the form (χ_pγ_{v,f} ∗; 0 γ_{v,f}), does not depend on f from the single fact that the residual representation of the forms in the localisation at m does not depend on f. Correction: The deduction needs, for v ∈ Σ not above p, that N(v) ≢ −1 mod p or that ρ̄_m|D_v is not a direct sum of two unramified characters; otherwise the Hecke algebra has to be enlarged by the operator of a uniformiser of D_v^×, or the lemma stated for each of its eigenvalues. Reason: The two candidates are γ and γη, η the unramified quadratic character. Their residual semisimplifications are {χ̄_pγ̄, γ̄} and {χ̄_pγ̄η̄, γ̄η̄}. Since p > 2, η̄ ≠ 1, and the two sets coincide exactly when χ̄_p|D_v = η̄, that is when v ∤ p and N(v) ≡ −1 mod p. Then a split ρ̄_m|D_v ≅ γ̄ ⊕ γ̄η̄ is compatible with both candidates, while a non-split one still singles out γ̄ as its unramified quotient. The Hecke algebra of the lemma contains only the operators at places outside S ⊇ Σ, so the maximal ideal does not see the sign. Whether forms of both signs actually occur in one localisation (as level raising suggests when N(v) ≡ −1 mod p) was not decided. Effect: the proof. Known correction: new.

## Boundaries and structure

Deligne–Serre's weight-one Artin representation has one owner, R19.1; ModularityAndLanglandsExtensions ML.1 and ClassicalSerreModularity R27.6 consume it. Weight-two special-fibre geometry is imported from R14.6. The all-Hilbert case is not narrowed to Carayol's geometry. Chenevier's theorem with a residually split determinant covers finite residue fields.

The strict family and its purity use R19.4 and R19.5, although R19.3 precedes them. The packet proposes a sub-layer R19.3b after R19.5 for these targets.

The early GL_n realisations AG2.1–AG2.2 of the Part II are imported for Blasius–Rogawski motives and for Sym²π, and the λ-adic character of an algebraic Hecke character is imported from its node in AG2.0; the converse for Hodge–Tate characters comes from R24.5/character-system. The Part II's late rank-two comparisons consume R19, so the packet proposes moving them to a consumer sub-layer to keep layer links acyclic. Saito's geometric weight theorem is requested from R34.6 on the geometric realisations only, without the coefficient-prime application, which belongs to R19.5.
